L425: ## 6.3. Comprehensive Clinical Pharmacology Review
L426: ## 6.3.1. General Pharmacology and Pharmacokinetic Characteristics
L427: ## Data:
L428: ## Summary of Pharmacokinetics
L429: Characterization of clinical serum pharmacokinetics, including assessment of distribution and elimination characteristics, dose linearity characterization, source of pharmacokinetic (PK)
L430: variability, and individual-level covariates for mosunetuzumab in patients with NHL are summarized below.
L431: ## **Key findings:**
L432: - Non-compartmental analyses (NCA) indicate that mosunetuzumab serum concentration reaches the maximal level (maximum drug concentration in serum [C_{max}]) at the end of infusion and declines in a bi-exponential fashion, with an apparent half-life (t_{1/2}) of approximately 3 to 10 days. The terminal half-life estimate was 16.1 days at steady state based on population pharmacokinetics (popPK) model simulations.
- The serum concentration-time data was well-described by a popPK model with two compartment and time-dependent clearance (CL), which was parameterized as an initial baseline clearance (CL_{base} \sim1.08 L/day) which transitions over time to a steady state clearance (CL_{ss} \sim0.584 L/day) with a transition half-life (HL_{trans}) of 16.3 days. The CL values are higher than the typical range of clearance of a typical IgG1 antibody, suggesting potential impact of target-mediated drug disposition (TMDD).
- The central volume of distribution (V1) was 5.49 L and peripheral volume of distribution (V2) was 6.17 L. The central volume of distribution of 5.49 L is larger than plasma volume, suggesting the potential impact of binding to immediately accessible targets following IV administration of mosunetuzumab.
- Mosunetuzumab exhibits linear and dose-proportional PK in the dose range studied (0.2 to 60 mg) and in the clinically active dose range (\ge1.2 mg).
- No clinically meaningful baseline covariates were found for mosunetuzumab pharmacokinetics requiring dose adjustments.Moderate to high inter-individual variability (IIV) was observed for mosunetuzumab key PK parameters (range from 18% to 86%).
- Significant baseline covariate effects include body weight on CL_{ss}, V1 and V2, albumin on CL_{base} and V1, sex on CL_{ss} and V1, tumor burden on CL_{ss}, and baseline anti-CD20 (aCD20) drug concentrations on CL_{base}. However, none of these covariates resulted in more than 20% change in mosunetuzumab pharmacokinetics exposure (area under the concentration-time curve from time 0 to 42 days, AUC_{0-42}), when evaluated at extreme values compared to the typical patient.
L433: ## Pharmacokinetics in Special Populations
L434: See 6.2.2.2.
L435: ## Material Comparability
L436: PopPK modeling indicate similar pharmacokinetics between the v0.1 (used in dose escalation cohorts) and v0.2 materials (used in the dose escalation and expansion cohorts, including pivotal B11 FL RP2D cohort).
L437: ## Exposure-Response Relationships
L438: The exposure-response (ER) relationships for efficacy were characterized based on investigator (INV)-assessed clinical objective response data from 159 patients with R/R FL who had received more than two prior therapies and treated with IV administration of mosunetuzumab in Study GO29781. The ER relationships for safety were assessed based on 439 PK-evaluable patients with R/R NHL receiving IV administration of mosunetuzumab from Study GO29781. Key findings are summarized below.
L439: ## **ER analyses for biomarker:**
L440: - The ER analyses for pharmacodynamic biomarker support evidence of downstream pharmacologic activity and indicate on-study change in IFN-\gamma to be associated with increasing maximal receptor occupancy (RO_{max}) following Cycle 1 Day 1 dose (“1st Dose”). However, the ER relationships following the subsequent Cycle 1 mosunetuzumab doses remained relatively flat. The shift (i.e., reduction) in ER relationship is consistent with the mitigation of acute cytokine release from T cells upon repeat dosing of mosunetuzumab. The Cycle-1 step doses of 1 mg and 2 mg are pharmacodynamically active and CRS-mitigating. The ER and peripheral IFN-\gamma data support the use of Cycle 1 step-up dosing regimen to mitigate acute cytokine-driven toxicity of mosunetuzumab.
L441: ## **ER analyses for efficacy:**
L442: - The ER analyses indicate that clinical responses (complete response rate [CRR] and objective response rate [ORR]) increase with increasing mosunetuzumab exposure (AUC_{0-42}) and approach a plateau estimated by the model as the maximal clinical responses of 68.6 % and 81.4% for CRR and ORR, respectively.
- ER analysis showed a trend of longer median PFS with increased mosunetuzumab exposure (AUC_{0-42}).
- The duration of response (DOR) (for OR and CR) was similar at the 1/2/60/30 mg dose compared to the 1/2/13.5 mg dose. ER analysis showed a trend of longer DOR (for OR and CR) with increasing exposure (AUC_{0-42}).
L443: ## **ER analyses for safety:**
L444: - CRS following step-up dosing of mosunetuzumab is primarily low grade and the occurrence is mostly confined to Cycle 1. CRS events are less frequent at Cycle 2 and onwards.
- ER analyses indicate a positive E-R relationship where the occurrence of Grade \ge 2 CRS increases modestly with higher maximal receptor occupancy (RO_{max}) values. ER analyses by dosing groups (Group A – fixed dosing regimen; Group B: Cycle-1 step-up dosing regimen) indicate that the exposure-dependent increase in Grade \ge 2 CRS observed at the lower dose range (0.05 to 2.8 mg) tested in Group A using the fixed dosing regimen was mitigated in Group B by Cycle 1 step-up regimen.
- CRS ER analyses (stratified by mosunetuzumab Cycle 1 Day 1 dose, Day 8 dose, and Day 15 dose) indicated higher RO_{max} values to be associated with modest increasing rates of
L445: Grade \ge 2 CRS upon the initial doses in Cycle 1. However, E-R trends upon weekly repeat dosing in Cycle 1 suggest a pattern of similar/ decreased CRS risk at comparatively higher exposures in subsequent doses, thus supporting the step-up regimen approach as an effective CRS mitigation strategy.
L446: - The Cycle 1 step-up dosing schedule mitigated the acute CRS risks and resulted in a favorable safety profile with predicted low frequency of Grade \ge 2 CRS (less than 12%) at the RP2D/intended registration dose and schedule of 1/2/60/30 mg.
- Dosing of mosunetuzumab following the step-up dosing regimen does not lead to exposure-dependent increases for Grade \ge 3 neutropenia, Grade \ge 3 infections or infestations, any Grade \ge 3 adverse events (AEs), or any Grade \ge 1 AEs. Higher mosunetuzumab exposure does not lead to an earlier onset of AEs.
L447: ## Immunogenicity
L448: The potential of mosunetuzumab to induce an immune response is being assessed in the ongoing clinical Study GO29781. All patients in the study are being monitored as required at regular intervals for the development of anti-mosunetuzumab antibodies.
L449: As of the ADA data cutoff date of 4 December 2020, there was no ADA detected in 418 ADA evaluable patients who received mosunetuzumab single agent IV treatments in Study GO29781. The observation of no ADA based on the available data is consistent with the expected mechanism of action of B cell killing.
L450: ## The Applicant's Position:
L451: Collectively, the PK and E-R analyses support intended registration dose and schedule of 1/2/60/30 mg in patients with R/R FL and support a positive benefit-risk profile.
L452: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L453: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. Mosunetuzumab PK properties, E-R relationships and immunogenicity assessment have been well characterized and support the proposed dose and schedule of 1/2/60/30 mg in patients with R/R FL.
L454: - PK: see Section 6.2.1 and 6.2.2.2.
- E-R for Efficacy:The E-R efficacy analyses indicated that clinical responses (CR, ORR) increased with increasing mosunetuzumab exposure (AUC_{0-42}) and approached a plateau at the proposed dosage of 1/2/60/30 mg. The covariate analysis indicated that patients with larger tumors or with high predose anti-CD20 antibody concentrations are predicted to have a lower probability to attain CR, though still expected to be higher than 50% for CR.
- The E-R efficacy analyses indicated a longer median PFS with increased mosunetuzumab exposure (AUC_{0-42}), and a longer median PFS at the 1/2/60/30 mg dose (B11 cohort, RP2D) compared to the 1/2/13.5 mg dose (B7 cohort).
L455: - E-R for Safety:No positive E-R relationships were observed for any AEs, any grade \ge 3 AEs, any serious AE, Grade &gt;3 neutropenia, Grade \ge 3 infections or infestations, neurological AE (NAE) or DI-CCNAE (driving-impacting cognition or consciousness neurologic adverse event).
- E-R for safety analysis support the proposed step-up dosing regimen of 1/2/60/30 mg to mitigate the risk of Grade \ge 2 CRS.
- ER for Pharmacodynamic Biomarker:E-R analyses for pharmacodynamic biomarker (IFN-\gamma) is acceptable and supportive. In addition, in response to FDA information request, E-R analyses for IL-6 was also provided. The E-R relationship for IL-6 follows a similar trend to IFN-\gamma. See details in Section 20.4.5.
- Immunogenicity:No ADAs were detected in 418 ADA evaluable patients who received mosunetuzumab single agent IV administration in Study GO29781.
L456: See Section 20.4.2 for additional PK parameters for each dose cohort studied in group A and B in study GO29781. See Section 20.4.4 and Section 20.4.5 for details regarding the Population PK and E-R analysis.
L457: ## 6.3.2. Clinical Pharmacology Questions
L458: ## 6.3.2.1 Does the clinical pharmacology program provide supportive evidence of effectiveness?
L459: ## **Data:**
L460: Exposure-response for clinical CRR and ORR were characterized in 159 patients with R/R FL across a 300-fold dose range (i.e., 0.2 to 60 mg) and across 600-fold of AUC_{0-42} values (0.400-251 day\cdot\mug/mL), to properly inform the characterization of the ER relationships. Clinical response increases in an exposure-dependent manner and plateaus at the AUC_{0-42} values of approximately 92.7 and 38.4 day\cdot\mug/mL corresponding to the estimated E_{90} for CRR and ORR, respectively. The intended registration dose and schedule of 1/2/60/30 mg is predicted to achieve PK exposures at the plateau of the E-R curves for CRR and ORR and thus maximizes the therapeutic potential in the patient population. The proposed 1/2/60/30 mg leads to higher predicted CRR and ORR compared to the lower dose of 1/2/13.5 mg, which is consistent with the clinical observations (INV-assessed: CRR/ORR = 60%/77.8% and 47.8%/63.0% for B11 and B7 cohorts, respectively; IRF-assessed: CRR/ORR=60%/80% and 45.7%/67.4% for B11 and B7 cohorts, respectively).
L461: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L462: Based on totality of data, the Applicant believes the mosunetuzumab clinical pharmacology program together with the efficacy results from Study GO29781 provides sufficient evidence of
L463: effectiveness at the intended registration dose and schedule of 1/2/60/30 mg.
L464: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L465: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. Refer to Section 20.4.5 for details regarding the E-R analysis for efficacy.
L466: 6.3.2.2 Is the proposed dosing regimen appropriate for the general patient population for which the indication is being sought?
L467: ## **Data:**
L468: See Section 6.2.2.2
L469: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L470: The intended registration dose and schedule of 1/2/60/30 mg for mosunetuzumab is appropriate based on effectiveness (see Section 6.3.2.1), Cycle 1 step-up dosing mitigation of CRS, exposure-response for safety, and no dose adjustment for mosunetuzumab needed for the general patient population for which the indication is being sought i.e., R/R FL \ge2 Prior Therapies.
L471: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L472: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position that the proposed dose and schedule of 1/2/60/30 mg for mosunetuzumab is appropriate for the general population for which the indication is being sought. See detailed discussion in Section 6.2.2.1.
L473: 6.3.2.3 Is an alternative dosing regimen or management strategy required for subpopulations based on intrinsic patient factors?
L474: ## **Data:**
L475: See Section 6.2.2.2.
L476: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L477: There is no alternative dosing regimen or management strategy required for subpopulations based on intrinsic patient factors.
L478: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L479: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position that no dose adjustment is needed in subpopulations based on intrinsic patient factors.
L480: 6.3.2.4 Are there clinically relevant food-drug or drug-drug interactions, and what is the appropriate management strategy?
L481: ## **Data:**
L482: Mosunetuzumab is administered intravenously (bioavailability=1.0) and no food effect would be expected with parenteral administration.
L483: Physiologically-based pharmacokinetics (PBPK) modeling and simulations based on IL-6 and CYP3A4 interaction indicated a low risk of cytokine-mediated drug-drug interaction (DDI) potential of mosunetuzumab on cytochrome P450 (CYP) enzyme expression or activity. The predicted area under the concentration-time curve (AUC) and maximum concentration (C_{max}) ratios (with mosunetuzumab co-administration/without mosunetuzumab co-administration) for midazolam, a sensitive CYP3A substrate was 1.37 and 1.17, respectively. No dose adjustment is recommended for mosunetuzumab when co-dosing mosunetuzumab with small molecule drugs which are CYP3A substrates.
L484: The potential impact of baseline obinutuzumab (G) or rituximab (R) concentration on the PK and clinical response of mosunetuzumab was evaluated in the population PK and exposure-response (E-R) models based on study GO29781 including patients with R/R FL \ge2 prior therapies. Baseline G or R concentration was determined not to be a clinically meaningful covariate on PK and response. The lack of clinically meaningful changes in the clinical response of mosunetuzumab in the presence of residual R or G is consistent with expectations, given the indolent nature of the disease which allows for a broader time window for response. No dose adjustment is recommended for mosunetuzumab based on prior anti-CD20 therapies.
L485: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L486: There was no evidence for clinically relevant food-drug or drug-drug interactions for mosunetuzumab.
L487: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L488: Mosunetuzumab can cause transient release of cytokines that may potentially suppress CYP450 enzymes. Based on evaluation of the totality of data, FDA considers the risk of drug interactions to be low. FDA's conclusion is based on the following considerations:
L489: - The elevations of cytokines observed after mosunetuzumab doses are transient in nature and attenuate with repeat dosing.
- The highest drug-drug interaction risk is expected to occur from initiation of mosunetuzumab step-up dosing schedule up to 14 days after the second loading dose (60 mg) on Cycle 2 Day 1, and during and after CRS.
- Label recommends monitoring for toxicity or concentrations of drugs that are CYP450 substrates where minimal concentration changes may lead to serious adverse reactions.
L490: See Section 20.4.7 for detailed assessment of the PBPK modeling and simulations to support the above conclusions.

L512: ## 8 Statistical and Clinical Evaluation
L513: ## 8.1. Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
L514: ## 8.1.1. GO29781
L515: ## Trial Design
L516: ## **The Applicant's Description:**
L517: The clinical efficacy and safety results supporting this application are from a single ongoing multicenter, open-label, Phase I/II dose-escalation and dose-expansion study GO29781 of mosunetuzumab administered as a single agent (and in combination with atezolizumab) in patients with R/R hematologic malignancies expected to express CD20, including B-cell NHL and CLL.
L518: The initial patients enrolled were treated with escalating doses of single-agent mosunetuzumab administered IV on Day 1 of each 3-weekly (q3w) cycle (Group A fixed dosing schedule). Subsequent protocol amendments were implemented to expand the study to evaluate a different IV dosing schedule (Group B Cycle 1 step-up dosing), include a dose expansion phase to evaluate mosunetuzumab at a recommended expansion dose in specific B-cell NHL histologies, and introduce efficacy as a co-primary objective. Further dose escalation and expansion cohorts were also introduced to evaluate mosunetuzumab IV in combination with atezolizumab (Group E), and mosunetuzumab administered as a single agent by SC injection (Groups D and F). The study was re-classified from Phase I/Ib to Phase I/II to reflect the overall size of the study, and the study objective to investigate the safety, efficacy, and PK/PD of the recommended Phase II dose (RP2D) in expansion cohorts with defined NHL histologies (protocol v12, dated 23 February 2021).
L519: The GO29781 study enrolled adult patients with a history of a hematologic malignancy that is expected to express the CD20 antigen, including FL, MZL, DLBCL, PMBCL, transformed indolent NHL, MCL, Richter's transformation, and CLL/SLL. Confirmation of CD20 expression was not required during eligibility screening prior to enrollment and was evaluated retrospectively based on data from a central lab. Patients included in the pivotal R/R FL expansion cohort had to have histologically-documented Grade 1-3a FL, measurable disease, must have relapsed after or failed to respond to at least two prior lines of systemic therapy and must have received prior treatment of an anti-CD20 directed therapy and an alkylating agent.
L520: Results to support this application are from Groups A and B only, which evaluated mosunetuzumab as a single agent administered by IV infusion.
L521: After completion of the first eight dose escalation cohorts, further enrolment into Group A was stopped beyond 2.8 mg (the highest assessed dose in Group A) to prioritize assessment of other dosing schedules and routes of mosunetuzumab administration. The Group B Cycle 1 step-up dosing schedule evaluated single-agent mosunetuzumab administered IV as incremental doses on C1D1, C1D8, and C1D15 dose levels followed by q3w dosing on Day 1 of subsequent cycles. The dose escalation evaluated 11 dose levels (ranging from 0.4/1/2.8 mg to 1/2/60 mg [B1-B11]), according to a standard 3+3 design. The Group B dose-expansion stage was designed to obtain additional safety, tolerability, PK, and clinical efficacy data with single agent mosunetuzumab administered IV by Cycle 1 step-up dosing in one or more dose expansion cohorts at dose levels at or below the MTD (which was not reached). The selected RP2D, which is also the intended registration dose, and schedule was 1/2/60/30 mg (i.e., 1 mg on C1D1, 2 mg on C1D8, 60 mg on C1D15 and C2D1, and 30 mg for C3D1 and subsequent q3w cycles), and was chosen based on cumulative data and additional analyses on exposure-efficacy and exposure-safety relationships (see 6.2.2.1). The RP2D and schedule was evaluated in four indication-specific expansion cohorts; R/R FL, R/R DLBCL/trFL, R/R MCL, and R/R Richter's transformation.
L522: Patients who achieved a CR after 8 cycles received a maximum of 8 cycles, and patients who achieved a PR or maintained stable disease after 8 cycles could receive a maximum of 17 cycles. Patients who achieved a CR with initial single agent mosunetuzumab treatment and subsequently relapsed (PD) could receive re-treatment with mosunetuzumab for at least 8 additional cycles. Patients were withdrawn from treatment due to disease progression or relapse, unacceptable toxicity, use of NALT, patient or investigator decision or pregnancy. Mosunetuzumab dose could be interrupted (delayed) for specific treatment emergent AEs (e.g. DLTs, Grade 3 or serious AE) for up to 2 weeks in order to recover from the toxicity. The mosunetuzumab dose could be continued with or without dose reduction. Patients who completed or discontinued initial study treatment without disease progression continued to be monitored, including regularly scheduled tumor assessments in post-initial treatment follow-up. Patients who discontinued treatment due to disease progression or experienced disease progression in post-initial treatment follow-up could be followed for survival and subsequent anti-cancer therapy.
L523: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L524: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the study design. Study GO29781 is an open-label, multicenter, multi-cohort study that included a cohort of patients with relapsed or refractory follicular lymphoma who have received at least two prior therapies, including an anti-CD20 monoclonal antibody and an alkylating agent. Patients were treated with mosunetuzumab at the recommended dose and schedule (1/2/60/30 mg).
L525: ## Study Endpoints
L526: ## The Applicant's Description:
L527: The primary efficacy endpoint of IRF-assessed CR rate according to standard NHL response criteria (Cheson et al. 2007) was introduced when efficacy became a co-primary objective of the study at the time the study expanded to become a Phase Ib design (Protocol v4, dated 14 June 2017). This amendment was prospectively planned and implemented before any patient had been enrolled into the Group B histology-specific expansion cohorts.
L528: CR rate was chosen as the primary endpoint as it is clinically relevant, directly attributable to drug effect and not the natural history of the disease, is based on objective and quantitative assessments, and appropriate for a single arm study. Several lines of evidence indicate that CR rate is reasonably likely to predict clinical benefit in patients with FL to support accelerated approval. The quality (depth) of response is associated with better outcomes both in the first-line (Shi et al. 2017; Trotman et al. 2018) and R/R setting (Liu et al. 2020) and two meta-analyses of large databases of published trial data suggest that CR rate may be a potential surrogate for PFS in FL (Zhu et al. 2017; Mangal et al. 2018). Additionally, the NCI Lymphoma Clinical Trials Planning Meeting Follicular Subcommittee recommended in trials for patients with high-risk R/R FL, the primary endpoints be PET-negative CR at the end of therapy and/or continuous CR at 30 months (Maddocks et al. 2017) (see Section 1.7.3. of the Clinical Overview, Module 2.5 for details).
L529: In order to minimize the potential for investigator bias and ensure objectivity in interpretation of results by the investigator in this open-label study, the primary endpoint was based on tumor assessments reviewed independently by an IRF.
L530: As GO29781 is a single arm study, the primary endpoint of IRF-assessed CR rate achieved by patients in the pivotal B11 FL RP2D cohort was compared statistically (exact binomial test with two-sided alpha level of 5%) with an historical control CR rate assumed to be (b)/(4)\% for the R/R FL \ge 2 prior therapies population based on the CR rate reported for copanlisib in the CHRONOS-1 study (Dreyling et al. 2017).
L531: Secondary efficacy endpoints evaluated in Study GO29781 were CR rate by INV, ORR, DOCR, DOR, PFS (all by IRF and INV), OS and PRO measures. The analysis of these endpoints was prospectively planned in the Statistical Analysis Plan (SAP; see below).
L532: This primary analysis of efficacy was conducted when both of the following were met:
L533: - At least approximately 6 months after the last patient in (LPI) in the B11 FL RP2D and B11 DLBCL/trFL RP2D expansion cohorts, whichever was later.
- Efficacy evaluable population included approximately 80 patients in this cohort for each histology.
L534: Hypothesis testing for the primary endpoint was conducted once only at the time of the primary analysis, based on a clinical cutoff date of 15 March 2021.
L535: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L536: The pre-specified primary efficacy endpoint was CR rate per independent review facility per the 2007 international working group criteria (Cheson 2007). However, the FDA uses overall response rate as an intermediate endpoint reasonable likely to predict clinical benefit, supported by durability. In addition, the FDA disagrees with the Applicant's statement that CR rate may be a potential surrogate for PFS in patients with follicular lymphoma. The use of CR rate in patients with relapsed or refractory follicular lymphoma is not an established surrogate endpoint and has not been used as an intermediate endpoint to support a regulatory determination in follicular lymphoma. Of note, complete response rate at 30 months is an established surrogate endpoint in patients with previously untreated follicular lymphoma.
L537: The proposed historical control CR rate of (b)(4)\% was not agreed upon with FDA. Comparison of response rates from single-arm trials against a historical rate is challenging because the historical benchmark is difficult to verify. Due to differences in population, trial design and other possible unknown confounders, the historical estimation is likely subject to biases which cannot be resolved. Consequently, the proposal for a hypothesis test or comparison against (b)(4)\% is not adequate for inferential purposes but instead is considered as supportive only, and the corresponding p-value is nominal.
L538: ## **Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments**
L539: ## **The Applicant's Description:**
L540: The analyses conducted were prospectively planned and described in the Statistical Analysis Plan (SAP, dated 18 December 2020) which was reviewed by the FDA (Type B Pre-BLA Content and Format meeting, 21 January 2021) prior to the CCOD for the primary analysis. The SAP was amended (v2, 21 May 2021) to revise censoring rules for time-to-event endpoints for events occurring after NALT (at the recommendation of the Agency). There were no changes made to the planned analyses of the primary and key secondary endpoints according to the SAP after the database snapshot for either the primary, or updated analyses.
L541: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L542: The primary analysis for efficacy was conducted on the B11 pivotal cohort, which includes 90 patients with relapsed or refractory FL treated at the proposed registrational dose (i.e., 1/2/60/30 mg) in Group B of Study GO29781.
L543: Response rates (ORR and CR) are summarized with 95% Clopper-Pearson Confidence Interval (CI) in the B11 pivotal cohort. Patients with missing/no response assessments are counted as non-responders.
L544: Duration of response and duration of CR are summarized with the Kaplan-Meier method in the B11 pivotal cohort patients who achieved ORR and CR.
L545: ## Protocol Amendments
L546: ## The Applicant's Description:
L547: The initial GO29781 study protocol, dated 23 April 2015 was amended 11 times up to the version current at the time of the CCOD for the updated analysis supporting the application (v12, dated 11 March 2021). The key amendments relevant to the evaluation of efficacy and safety in cohorts of patient receiving mosunetuzumab IV monotherapy were as follows:
L548: - Group B to evaluate Cycle 1 step-up doing to minimize potential toxicity with mosunetuzumab IV was introduced (v2, 13 August 2016)
- Further enrolment into the dose escalation of Group A (q3w fixed dosing) was stopped beyond 2.8 mg to prioritize assessment of other dosing schedules and routes of administration (v4, 14 June 2017)
- The efficacy of mosunetuzumab using the Group B dosing schedule in patients with R/R FL as measured by IRF-assessed CR rates was added as a co-primary objective of the study. In addition, secondary objectives were expanded to include PRO-based assessment of HRQoL in the NHL expansion cohorts (v6, 27 November 2017)
- Several amendments to the protocol were made to incorporate additional risk monitoring and mitigation plans based on evolving clinical data including additional guidance provided for mitigation, management and reporting of CRS events, and neurologic events.
L549: None of the modifications to the protocol were considered to have an impact on the integrity of the trial or the interpretation of the efficacy or safety data.
L550: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L551: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's summary of the key protocol amendments for Study GO29781.

The appropriate Ethics Committees and Institutional Review Boards reviewed and approved this study.

L563: ## **Patient Disposition**
L564: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L565: Between 15 September 2015 (first-patient enrolled) and 27 August 2021 (the CCOD for the updated analysis), 447 patients were enrolled into Groups A (dose escalation; n=33) and B (dose escalation and expansion; n=414) to receive mosunetuzumab as a single agent by IV infusion. Patients were enrolled at 40 sites in 7 countries (Australia, Canada, Germany, S. Korea, Spain, United Kingdom, and US).
L566: In Group B, 218 patients received the RP2D/intended registration dose and schedule (the primary safety population), including 90 patients with R/R FL \ge2 prior therapies (primary efficacy population).
L567: As of the CCOD, none of the patients in the pivotal B11 FL RP2D cohort continued to receive initial treatment, 41 patients were in post-initial treatment follow-up (without disease progression), 35 patients were in survival follow-up (including one patient who received re-treatment), 2 patients were active on first re-treatment, and 12 patients had discontinued from the study.
L568: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L569: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. As shown in Table 18, no patients in the pivotal B11 FL RP2D cohort and two in the B11 RP2D cohort were still on active treatment by the data cutoff. The major reason for treatment discontinuation was progressive disease, which accounted for 28% and 50% of patients in the pivotal B11 FL RP2D cohort and B11 RP2D cohort, respectively.

L572: ## Protocol Violations/Deviations
L573: ## The Applicant's Position:
L574: A total of 150 major protocol deviations were reported in 111 of the 414 patients (26.8%) in Group B. By category, the most common (&gt;5% patients) major protocol deviations were 'failure to report AEs, SAEs, AESIs, DLTs, and pregnancy to the IEC/sponsor/regulatory authorities' and 'incomplete/missing/out-of-window tumor-related assessments'.
L575: One patient with a non-hematological malignancy of melanoma was enrolled in error into the B11 DLBCL/trFL RP2D cohort. The patient's exclusion from the efficacy-evaluable population did not affect the results of the primary analysis in R/R FL patients supporting this application. The patient was included in the safety evaluable population because she received one dose of mosunetuzumab.
L576: In the pivotal B11 FL RP2D efficacy cohort, 32 of the 90 patients had major protocol deviations and all were included in the efficacy analyses. The major deviations relating to tumor assessments, eligibility criteria, or treatment compliance were as follows:
L577: 97
L578: *Version date: January 2020 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)*
L579: *Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as "Data" and "The Applicant's Position" are completed by the Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.*
L580: - Eleven patients had major deviations categorized as incomplete/missing/out-of-window tumor-related assessments as a bone marrow biopsy was not performed at screening and/or within 42 days of the radiographic complete response to confirm a CR. In each case, the CR was downgraded to a PR at that tumor assessment visit, and the efficacy endpoints of CR rate and duration of CR reflect this conservative assessment.
- One patient in the B11 FL RP2D cohort with negative serologic test results for acute or chronic hepatitis B (HBV) infection at screening received their first dose of mosunetuzumab prior to a positive hepatitis B virus (HBV) PCR result on the same day. Per protocol, patients with known active infections were to be excluded from the study. HBV PCR negativity was achieved and maintained during mosunetuzumab treatment with concomitant antiviral treatment and the patient achieved PR as their best overall response.
- One patient received 10 cycles of mosunetuzumab treatment despite achieving a CR after 8 cycles.
- One patient had a delayed treatment cycle due to the COVID-19 pandemic after a CR was achieved. Another patient had an incomplete infusion due to the COVID-19 pandemic; the patient completed the rest of the treatment cycles and achieved a CR. Two other patients were administered mosunetuzumab at a faster infusion rate than recommended per protocol. Both patients achieved CR as their best overall response.
L581: A total of 11 major protocol deviations due to the COVID-19 pandemic were reported in 10 patients in Group B overall. None of the 5 major protocol deviations due to COVID-19 in the pivotal B11 FL RP2D cohort were attributed to incomplete/missing/out-of-window tumor assessments and therefore, response rates and the TTE analyses are not expected to be impacted by the missing data due to the COVID-19 pandemic.
L582: Overall, none of the major protocol deviations were considered to have impacted the monitoring of patient safety, the study outcome, or the interpretation of the study results.
L583: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L584: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's summary of protocol deviations.

L591: ## The Applicant's Position:
L592: The majority of patients in the pivotal B11 FL RP2D cohort were White (82.2%) (although almost all race groups were represented) and male (61.1%). The median age was 60 years (range:
L593: 29-90 years) and 33.3% were \ge 65 years. Per protocol, all patients were ECOG performance status 0 (58.9%) or 1 (41.1%).
L594: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L595: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's summary of demographic characteristics in the FL cohort of 90 patients. As noted in the Applicant's position, the majority of patients were White (82%) with limited representation from other races and ethnicities. In particular, there was an underrepresentation of Black or African American patients and Hispanic or Latino patients, who comprised only 4% and 8% of the primary FL population, respectively. In Section 2.1, the Applicant stated that the incidence of FL in Black or African American patients was 4%. This incidence was based on 70,882 patients with follicular lymphoma. Of these patients with follicular lymphoma, an incidence of 10% in Hispanic or Latino patients was observed. The FL cohort from study GO29781 has a sample size of 90 patients, therefore the similar incidence of Black or African American and Hispanic or Latino patients is influenced by the small sample size of the FL cohort.

L603: Most patients in the pivotal B11 FL RP2D cohort had advanced (Ann Arbor Stage III/IV) disease at study entry (76.7%). L608: Accountability and study treatment compliance, as required per protocol, were assessed by review of the pharmacy drug dispensing records and administration logs.
L609: The most frequent concomitant treatments ongoing at baseline were either standard premedications for lymphoma patients who receive anti-cancer treatment (the most common being allopurinol) or were permitted medications to treat previous and concurrent medical conditions. The most frequent medications started after baseline were protocol-recommended premedications that patients were to receive prior to treatment with mosunetuzumab IV monotherapy (e.g. corticosteroids, antipyretics, and/or antihistamines, and uric acid reducing agents), hydration/electrolytes, and permitted concomitant therapy to manage adverse events (immunostimulants, e.g. G-CSFs, anti-infective prophylaxis for viral, fungal, bacterial, or pneumocystis infections, RBC and platelet transfusions).

L612: ## **Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint (Including Sensitivity Analyses)**
L613: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L614: The primary efficacy endpoint of IRF-assessed CR rate in the pivotal B11 FL RP2D cohort was met at the time of the primary analysis based on a CCOD of 15 March 2021. The CR rate with mosunetuzumab IV monotherapy at the RP2D at that time (57.8% (95% CI: 46.9, 68.1) was statistically significantly greater than the historical control CR rate of (b)/(4)% for patients with R/R FL who have received at least 2 prior therapies (exact binomial test, two-sided p value &lt;0.0001).
L615: In the updated analysis based on a CCOD of 27 August 2021, the CR rate as assessed by IRF in the B11 FL RP2D cohort was 60.0% (95% CI: 49.1, 70.2) (54/90 patients).
L616: A summary of the efficacy results for the B11 FL RP2D expansion cohort at the time of the updated efficacy analysis is shown in the table below.

L619: Response rates, DOR and PFS are based on tumor assessments with or without 18F-PET according to Cheson et al.

L622: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L623: The comparison of response rates from a single-arm trial against a historical rate is challenging because such a historical benchmark is difficult to verify and needs to be carefully reviewed and agreed upon prior to conducting any hypothesis test. Therefore, the hypothesis test or comparison against the historical control CR rate of (b)(4)% is considered as supportive only, and the corresponding p-value is nominal.
L624: Although CR rate was prespecified as the primary endpoint in the protocol and statistical analysis plan, FDA considers the intermediate endpoint of ORR and duration of response as assessed by an independent review facility according to standard criteria for NHL (Cheson 2007) to support substantial evidence of efficacy. In addition, the Applicant did not submit data (trial-level or patients-level) to support the use of CR rate as an intermediate endpoint to establish efficacy.
L625: As noted above, the Applicant based the efficacy hypothesis testing on a historical CR rate. In single-arm trials, the FDA generally uses these historically based calculations for sample size determination and does not use the demonstration of statistical significance as the ultimate determination of effectiveness. In NHL, the observed ORR with a 95% CI and duration of response are used in the overall assessment of efficacy in the context of the observed safety findings to inform the benefit-risk of a given product.
L626: For the B11 FL RP2D expansion cohort, the ORR rate is 80% (95% CI: 70.3%, 87.7%) and the estimated median DOR is 22.8 months (95% CI: 9.7, not estimable). The primary efficacy results were able to be reproduced by the statistical reviewer.
L627: Among 72 patients who achieved CR or PR, median time to response was 1.4 months (range: 1.1, 8.9); the majority of these responders (94%) achieved their response by 4 months since the start of treatment and only 6% responded after 4 months. Among 54 patients who achieved CR, median time to response was 3 months (range: 1.2, 18.9).
L628: For subgroup analysis accompanying the primary analysis in Table 21, see Section "Additional Analyses Conducted on the Individual Trial" below.
L629: ## **Data Quality and Integrity**
L630: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L631: No meaningful concerns are anticipated in the quality and integrity of the submitted datasets; these were sufficiently complete to allow for a thorough review of efficacy. Furthermore, no meaningful data integrity concerns were reported following completion of site inspections by
L632: the Applicant. The Applicant submitted a Quality Briefing Document with Rolling Part 2 of the BLA, which provides a summary of quality evidence generated to support the Quality Strategy developed for the mosunetuzumab program.
L633: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L634: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. In general, the data quality of the study appeared acceptable with no errors identified for the major study endpoints.
L635: ## **Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints**
L636: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L637: ## **Investigator-assessed CR rate**
L638: Investigator-assessed CR rate in the B11 FL RP2D cohort was 60.0% (95% CI: 49.1, 70.2) the same as the IRF-assessed CR rate. Concordance between IRF and Investigator on whether a patient achieved a CR was 93.3%.
L639: ## **Objective Response Rate**
L640: The objective response rate (ORR; PR or CR) by IRF assessment was 80.0% (95% CI: 73.0, 87.7) and consistent with the Investigator-assessed ORR rate of 77.8% (95% CI: 67.8, 85.9). Concordance between IRF and investigator on whether a patient achieved a CR or PR was 95.5%.
L641: ## **Duration of Complete Response**
L642: Of 54 patients in the pivotal B11 FL RP2D cohort who achieved a CR as assessed by the IRF, 16 patients (29.6%) subsequently had disease progression by the time of the CCOD. The median duration of complete response (DOCR) was not estimable. The K-M -estimated event-free rate among complete responders at 12 and 18 months after the first CR was 71.4% and 63.7%, respectively. Similar results were observed for DOCR by investigator assessment, with 80.4% and 66.6% of complete responders being event-free at 12 and 18 months, respectively, after the first CR (Table 21).
L643: ## **Duration of Response**
L644: The median duration of follow-up for DOR (by IRF) estimated using the reverse K-M method was 14.9 months (95% CI: 13.4, 16.6). At the CCOD, of the responders, 87.5% had either ongoing responses of at least 9 months or had progressed or died, 9.7% had DOR of &lt;9 months but no longer had tumor assessments, and the remaining 2.8% had ongoing responses which continued to be assessed.
L645: Of the 72 patients in the B11 FL RP2D cohort who achieved a response (CR or PR) as determined by the IRF, 29 patients (40.3%) had subsequently progressed or died. The K-M estimated median DOR was 22.8 months (95% CI: 9.7, NE), however this estimate was based on &lt;10% of
L646: responders remaining at risk and therefore may not be robust. Among responders, the event-free rates at 12 and 18 months after the first response were 61.8% and 56.9%, respectively. Similar results were observed for DOR by investigator assessment, with 64.8% and 62.5% of responders being event-free at 12 and 18 months, respectively, after the first response (Table 21). Concordance between the IRF and the investigator in DOR was 74.2%.
L647: In the subgroup of responders in the B11 FL RP2D cohort who achieved a CR by IRF assessment (54 patients), an estimated 76.4% (95% CI: 64.6, 88.1) maintained their response at 12 months after the first response. For DOR in patients who achieved a CR by investigator assessment, 84.3% were event-free at 12 months.
L648: ## Progression-free Survival
L649: At the time of the CCOD, 46.7% of patients in the B11 FL RP2D cohort had an IRF-assessed PFS event. The K-M estimated median PFS was 17.9 months (95% CI: 10.1, NE). The 12-month PFS event-free rate was 57.7% (95% CI: 46.9, 68.4). Overall, consistent results were observed with INV-assessed PFS.
L650: ## Overall Survival
L651: Survival data was not mature at the CCOD, with 8 deaths reported (8.9%) in the B11 FL RP2D cohort. The K-M estimated 12- and 18-month survival rates were 93.0% (95% CI: 87.6, 98.4) and 89.6% (95% CI: 82.5, 96.6), respectively.
L652: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L653: In general, the FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. As noted in the Applicant's position, the median DOR for ORR was 22.8 months, however this estimate was based on &lt;10% of responders remaining at risk. This is coupled with the median follow-up for DOR of 14.9 months. Therefore, to provide additional context for DOR, the landmark assessment of continued response at 12 months with 62% and at 18 months with 57% is supportive of durability. Additionally, FDA considers the time to event endpoints, including progression-free survival and overall survival, as not interpretable in single-arm trials. Therefore, the corresponding analysis of progression-free survival and overall survival results presented in Table 21 are considered exploratory analyses.
L654: ## Dose/Dose Response
L655: ## The Applicant's Position:
L656: The Exposure-Response (E-R) relationships for efficacy were characterized based on INV-assessed clinical objective response data from 159 patients with R/R FL \ge 2 prior therapies who were treated with single agent mosunetuzumab by IV administration in Study GO29781 (see Exposure-Response Report No. 1112149). These patients received mosunetuzumab doses
L657: ranging from 0.2 to 1/2/60/30 mg (7 patients from Group A q3w fixed-dose escalation cohorts and 152 patients from Group B step-up dosing cohort), yielding a 600-fold range in mosunetuzumab exposures (AUC_{0-42}). The E-R analyses indicate that clinical responses (CR rate and ORR) increase with increasing mosunetuzumab exposure (AUC_{0-42}) and approach a plateau estimated by the model as the maximal clinical responses of 68.6% and 81.4% for CR rate and ORR, respectively. The model predictions for the proposed 1/2/60/30 mg regimen are at the plateau of the logistic regression E_{max} E-R curves, while lower doses had reduced CR rate and ORR predictions. The rationale for the intended registration dose and schedule of 1/2/60/30 mg based on integrated E-R analyses of efficacy and safety for Study GO29781 is summarized in Section 6.2.2.1.
L658: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L659: The FDA agrees with Applicant's position. Refer to Section 6.2, 6.3, and Section 20.4.5 for additional information.
L660: ## **Durability of Response**
L661: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L662: Objective responses occurring during mosunetuzumab IV monotherapy were durable, and extended beyond the fixed treatment duration of 8 or 17 q3w cycles (see Duration of Response under Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints). With median follow-up for DOR of 14.9 months, the 18-month event-free rate was 56.9% (95% CI: 44.1, 69.6) for all responders, and 70.2% (95% CI: 56.7, 83.8) for patients who achieved a CR, indicating that patients who achieve a CR may have a higher probability of remission lasting at least 1.5 years.
L663: The durability of response to mosunetuzumab IV monotherapy is further supported by the results from 46 patients with R/R FL \ge 2 prior therapies treated at the B7 interim dose level (1.0/2.0/13.5 mg). The median follow-up for DOR in this lower dose cohort was 24.2 months [95% CI: 21.4, 28.3]), and the 18-month DOR rate (54.0% [95% CI: 35.5, 72.4]) was similar to the B11 FL RP2D cohort.
L664: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L665: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position and the results of duration of response and duration of CR presented above. The median follow-up time for DOR was 14.9 months (95% CI: 13.4, 16.8) among 72 responders who achieved CR or PR. The median follow-up time for duration of CR was 13.6 months (95% CI: 10.8, 16.3) among 54 responders who achieved CR.
L666: ## **Persistence of Effect**
L667: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L668: Response to mosunetuzumab IV monotherapy occurs early in the majority of responders (median time to response is 1.4 months for all responses and 3.0 months for CRs). The durability of responses beyond the fixed treatment period of 8 cycles (5.5 months) for patients with CR and 17 cycles (11.8 months) for patients with PR or SD provides evidence for the persistence of mosunetuzumab efficacy over time.
L669: The long-term persistency of the treatment effect of mosunetuzumab is not established, however the time-to-event endpoint K-M estimates at the primary efficacy analysis can be appreciated in the context of the disease setting and patient population under study. Although direct comparison of longer term benefit of mosunetuzumab IV treatment with an appropriate control is not possible in the confines of a single arm study and the lack of a comparator that would be considered the standard of care, the estimated 61.8% of patients who continued to benefit from remission after 12 months compares favorably with most approved therapies for patients with R/R FL who have received with \ge 2 prior therapies treated for whom median DOR is approximately a year or less (see Table 22). Furthermore, the 12-month PFS event-free rate of 57.7% (95% CI: 46.9, 68.4) compares favorably with published real-world estimates of median PFS ranging from 1-1.1 year for third-line patients (represented at 37.8% in the B11 FL RP2D cohort) decreasing to 0.5 years for sixth-line patients (Alperovich et al. 2016; Batlevi et al. 2020).
L670: A lack of tolerance effects is indicated by patients who received retreatment with mosunetuzumab. Of the 11 patients with R/R FL who received mosunetuzumab IV monotherapy retreatment for relapsed disease after an initial response of CR, 7 patients had an objective response to retreatment (5 CRs and 2 PRs) with duration of response to retreatment ranging from 5.3-20.7 months (ongoing at CCOD).
L671: As no ADAs were detected in any patient (see Section 8.2.9) there is no evidence to support the potential impact of neutralizing antibodies as a theoretical mode of tolerance or resistance to therapy.
L672: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L673: In general, the FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. However, the comparison of time to events endpoints (e.g., the percentages of patients who continued to be in remission at 12 months, and PFS at 12 months) between this single-arm trial and select published literature and the conclusion that such a comparison is in favor of mosunetuzumab is not well established. Generally, FDA does not consider any inferential procedures to evaluate time to event endpoints in single-arm trials and considers these results to be descriptive and hypothesis generating only.
L674: ## Efficacy Results – Secondary or exploratory COA (PRO) endpoints
L675: ## The Applicant's Position:
L676: PRO-based assessments of HRQoL in patients in the NHL expansion cohorts were introduced as a secondary endpoint in protocol v6, prior to the first patient being enrolled in the expansion cohorts.
L677: At baseline, patients in the R/R FL \ge 2 prior therapies expansion cohorts reported normal physical functioning and slightly elevated baseline fatigue symptom scores of the EORTC QLQ-C30 questionnaire, minimal to some burden of lymphoma-specific symptoms and concerns as measured by the FACT-Lym subscale, and a low level of health status impairment at baseline as measured by the EQ-5D-5L index utility scores and VAS. Changes from baseline scores at PRO assessment visits during the first 8 cycles of initial treatment were highly variable between patients, however mean changes from baseline by visit were not clinically meaningful indicating that baseline HRQoL and health status across the cohort populations as a whole was maintained during treatment with single-agent mosunetuzumab.
L678: In the B11 FL RP2D cohort, clinically meaningful improvements compared to baseline (\ge 10-point for EORT QLQ-C30 and \ge 3-point for FACT-Lym) were observed for physical functioning score in up to 15.8% of patients, fatigue score in up to 37.9% of patients, and for lymphoma symptoms in up to 44.0% of patients at specific PRO assessment visits during the 8 cycles of initial treatment with mosunetuzumab IV monotherapy.
L679: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L680: For B11 FL RP2D cohort, the compliance rates were &gt;85\% until Cycle 8 Day 1 for all three questionnaires and remained \ge 75\% afterwards. The FDA acknowledges the descriptive improvements for physical function, fatigue, and lymphoma symptoms. However, the FDA disagrees with the statement that the changes were clinically meaningful. For patient-reported disease improvement, a comparator arm along with a prespecified PRO endpoint and analysis plan are necessary for adequate interpretation. Additionally, the single-arm, open-label trial design limits the PRO data interpretation since a patients' knowledge of the treatment regimen may lead to systematic overestimation or underestimation of the treatment effect.
L681: ## **Additional Analyses Conducted on the Individual Trial**
L682: ## Data:
L683: ## *CR rate and ORR by Subgroup*
L684: Subgroup analyses of the CR rate and ORR by IRF assessment demonstrated the consistency of the treatment effect across relevant subpopulations defined by demographic (gender, age range categories, race/ethnicity, ECOG PS), baseline disease (bulky disease), prior treatment (number of prior systemic therapies, and refractory status to those prior treatments), and prognostic factors (FLIPI risk factor). CR rate and ORR for all subgroups were consistent with the response rates of the overall cohort. No major differences were observed among the
L685: subgroups. Specifically, consistent CR rates were observed in high-risk patient populations, including those with double refractory disease (50%), those who progressed within 24 months of initial treatment (57%), and those with disease refractory to PI3K inhibitors (50%).
L686: ## *Time to Response*
L687: Among the 72 patients who achieved an objective response (CR or PR) by IRF in the B11 FL RP2D cohort, the median time to first response was 1.4 months (range: 1.1–8.9 months), while median time to first CR in the 54 patients who achieved a CR (by IRF) was 3.0 months (range: 1.1–18.9 months).
L688: ## *Response Beyond 8 Cycles of Mosunetuzumab IV Monotherapy*
L689: A late initial response or deepening of a response was observed in some patients who had SD or PR per Investigator assessment at the end of Cycle 8 and who received further mosunetuzumab treatment beyond 8 cycles. Of the 16 patients who received &gt;8 cycles of mosunetuzumab IV monotherapy in the B11 FL RP2D cohort, 14 patients had tumor assessments beyond Cycle 8. Of these 14 patients, 6 patients who had a best response of SD or PR by the end of Cycle 8 had a late initial response or deepening of response (1 SD→PR, 5 PR→CR), 6 patients maintained SD (3) or their PR (3), and 1 patient progressed (SD→PD)(One other patient received mosunetuzumab doses beyond Cycle 8 despite having a CR at the end of Cycle 8 [captured as a major protocol deviation]). These data indicate the benefit of continuing mosunetuzumab IV monotherapy treatment beyond 8 cycles for patients who demonstrate disease control but have not yet achieved a complete response at Cycle 8.
L690: ## *Time to Next Treatment*
L691: Time to next treatment (TTNT) was defined as the time from the end of study treatment to the start of new anti-lymphoma treatment (NALT) or death. Thirty patients in the B11 FL RP2D cohort started NALT and 3 additional patients had died prior to starting NALT (33 events, 36.7%). The K-M-estimated median TTNT in the B11 FL RP2D cohort was not estimable. The 12-month event-free rate was 68.1% (95% CI: 58.3, 77.9).
L692: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L693: In general, the FDA agrees with the Applicant's position and the above results of subgroup analyses, time to response, response beyond 8 cycles of treatment, and time to next treatment. There were a limited number of patients (N = 16) that achieved a PR or SD with the initial 8 cycles of mosunetuzumab and received additional treatment (up to 17 cycles). As noted in the Applicant's position, of 14 patients with response assessments beyond Cycle 8, 6 patients had a deepening of response and 6 patients maintained their initial responses. In the context of limited patient numbers, this data provides some preliminary supportive evidence for continuation of treatment with mosunetuzumab beyond 8 cycles for patients that achieve a PR or SD with initial therapy.
L694: ## 8.1.3. Integrated Review of Effectiveness
L695: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L696: Not applicable. See Sections below.
L697: ## 8.1.4. Assessment of Efficacy Across Trials
L698: Not applicable as efficacy to support the application is based on the single pivotal study GO29781.
L699: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L700: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. The efficacy analysis is solely based on Study GO29781 and an integrated summary of efficacy was not included in the application. FDA notes that Study GO29781 was adequate to support the efficacy of the mosunetuzumab IV monotherapy in the indicated population.
L701: ## 8.1.5. Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness
L702: ## The Applicant's Position:
L703: Treatment with mosunetuzumab IV monotherapy induced durable complete remission in the majority of patients with R/R FL who had received at least two prior anti-lymphoma therapies, including patients who were refractory to multiple classes of prior treatment and with poor prognostic factors.
L704: In the primary efficacy analysis based on a CCOD of 15 March 2021, the IRF-assessed CR rate of 57.8% (95% CI: 46.9, 68.1) with mosunetuzumab IV monotherapy at the intended registration dose at that time was statistically significantly greater than a historical control CR rate of (b)(4)% for patients with R/R FL \ge2 prior therapies (exact binomial test, two-sided p value &lt;0.0001) based on CR rates reported for copanlisib in the CHRONOS-1 study (Dreyling et al. 2017), and compares favorably with other approved therapies (Table 22).
L705: Compared with therapies specifically approved to treat patients with R/R FL after 2 or 3 prior systemic therapies, the updated IRF-assessed CR rate based on a CCOD of 27 August 2021 (60.0% [95% CI: 49.1, 70.2]) and ORR 80.0% (95% CI: 70.3, 87.7) achieved by mosunetuzumab is greater than the respective response rates for the PI3K inhibitors idelalisib, copanlisib, duvelisib, and umbralisib, and the EZH2 inhibitor tazemetostat (CR range: 1–14%; ORR range: 34–69%; Table 22). The difference in response rate with mosunetuzumab appeared clinically meaningful as the lower bound 95% CI for the IRF-assessed CR rate (49.1%) and ORR (70.3%) exclude the reported point estimates for the respective response rates for the PI3K inhibitors. In addition, the K-M estimated median DOR in the pivotal B11 FL RP2D cohort of GO29781 was
L706: 22.8 months (95% CI: 9.7, NE), however this estimate was based on &lt;10% of responders remaining at risk and therefore may not be robust. Among responders, an estimated 56.9% remained in remission at 18 months after the first response, which compares favorably with median DORs ranging from 10 to 13 months for these approved therapies (Table 22). The proportion of patients who achieved CR and the durability of responses for patients with R/R FL \ge2 prior therapies treated with mosunetuzumab approach the results of axicabtagene ciloleucel, a CAR-T therapy (60% CR, 91% ORR; Table 22). While CAR-T therapies have shown efficacy, their use may be limited by treatment delay associated with the ex-vivo T-cell manipulation (median time from leukapheresis to product infusion 27 days [range 19 to 250 days]) and complex CAR-T manufacturing, which may be prohibitive in patients with rapidly progressing disease, as well as the high incidence of CRS and neurological adverse events (see Section 8.2.11). Of available therapies approved for first- and/or second-line FL which can be used as third-line and subsequent therapy, the CR rates for the combinations of rituximab plus lenalidomide (R-Len) and obinutuzumab plus bendamustine (G-B) in the third-line R/R FL setting (13-34% and 20%, respectively) are lower than for mosunetuzumab, while objective responses (57%-81% and 75%, respectively) are similar (Table 22; FDA Multidisciplinary Review and Evaluation of UKONIQ® and TAZVERIK®). However, the studies which supported the regular approval of R-Len (MAGNIFY and AUGMENT) and G-B (GADOLIN) enrolled earlier-line, less refractory, and less heavily pretreated patients with R/R FL than the GO29781 study. The highest response rates with R-Len were observed in AUGMENT, which enrolled patients without rituximab-refractory disease, compared to MAGNIFY (47% rituximab-refractory for patients with FL \ge2 prior therapies) whereas 79% of patients in GO29781 were refractory to a prior anti-CD20 therapy. Compared to R-Len in patients with R/R FL \ge2 prior therapies in MAGNIFY, the DOR for mosunetuzumab appears longer. The response rates for G-B in GADOLIN in the third-line setting were based on patients with rituximab-refractory FL who had received a median of 2 (range 2-10) prior therapies whereas in GO29781, patients had received a median of 3 (2-10) prior therapies.
L707: The deep and durable responses to single agent mosunetuzumab are clinically meaningful and may demonstrate advantages over approved therapies for the treatment of patients with R/R FL \ge2 prior therapies, a patient population with a high unmet medical need, who have rapidly progressing disease and need urgent disease control. To confirm the clinical benefit, the Applicant is conducting a randomized Phase III Study GO42909 to evaluate mosunetuzumab in combination with lenalidomide vs. rituximab in combination with lenalidomide in patients with FL after at least one line of systemic therapy with the primary efficacy endpoint of PFS. Study GO42909 is ongoing and enrolled the first patient on 27 October 2021.

L720: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L721: The efficacy of mosunetuzumab IV was based on GO29781, an open-label, multi-cohort, single-arm trial that included a cohort of 90 adult patients with relapsed or refractory follicular lymphoma who had received at least two prior therapies, including an anti-CD20 monoclonal antibody and an alkylating agent. The study excluded patients with active infections, history of autoimmune disease, prior allogeneic transplant, or any history of CNS lymphoma or CNS disorders.
L722: Patients received mosunetuzumab at step-up doses of 1 mg on Cycle 1 Day 1 and 2 mg on Cycle 1 Day 8, followed by 60 mg on Cycle 1 Day 15, and 60 mg on Cycle 2 Day 1, then 30 mg every 3 weeks in subsequent cycles. A treatment cycle was 21 days. Mosunetuzumab was administered for 8 cycles unless patients experienced progressive disease or unacceptable toxicity. After 8 cycles, patients with a complete response discontinued therapy; patients with a partial response or stable disease continued treatment up to 17 cycles, unless patients experienced progressive disease or unacceptable toxicity.
L723: The prespecified primary endpoint was complete response rate per independent review facility per the 2007 international working group criteria (Cheson 2007).
L724: Among the 90 patients with relapsed or refractory FL, the median age was 60 years (range: 29 to 90 years), 33% were 65 years of age or older, 61% were male, 82% were White, 9% were Asian, 4% were Black or African American, and 8% were Hispanic or Latino. A total of 77% of patients had Stage III-IV disease, 34% had bulky disease, and all patients had an ECOG performance status of 0 or 1. The median number of prior therapies was 3 (range: 2 to 10), with 38% receiving 2 prior therapies, 31% receiving 3 prior therapies, and 31% receiving more than 3 prior therapies.
L725: Seventy-nine percent of patients were refractory to prior anti-CD20 monoclonal antibody therapy, 53% were refractory to both anti-CD20 monoclonal antibody and alkylator therapy, 9% received prior rituximab plus lenalidomide therapy, 21% received prior autologous stem cell transplant, and 3% received prior CAR T therapy. Fifty-two percent of patients had progression of disease within 24 months of first systemic therapy.
L726: Although CR rate is pre-specified as the primary endpoint in the protocol and statistical analysis plan, FDA considers the intermediate endpoint of ORR and duration of response (DOR) as assessed by an independent review facility according to standard criteria for NHL (Cheson 2007) as the basis for efficacy. For the B11 FL RP2D expansion cohort of 90 patients with FL, the ORR rate was 80% (95% CI: 70.3%, 87.7%), with 60% achieving a complete response. The median DOR per Kaplan-Meier method was 22.8 months (95% CI: 9.7, not estimable) with a median DOR follow-up of 14.9 months. Given the median DOR follow-up and that the KM estimate was based on &lt;10% of responders remaining at risk, further support for DOR was assessed using the
L727: landmark assessments of continued response at 12 months with 62% and at 18 months with 57%.
L728: Based on the characteristics of the FL patient population enrolled in the expansion cohort of GO29781, the data support the determination that mosunetuzumab has clinically meaningful activity in patients with relapsed or refractory follicular lymphoma after two or more lines of systemic therapy. Some limitations of the efficacy data include the limited racial and ethnic representation with 82% White, 9% Asian 4% Black or African American, and 8% Hispanic or Latino. Further characterization of mosunetuzumab in a broader ethnic and racial population is warranted and informed a postmarketing requirement. In addition, the proposed treatment regimen is to continue treatment in patients that achieve a PR or SD within the initial 8 cycles of therapy. The data for treatment beyond 8 cycles is limited with only 16 patients. Although, 6 patients had a deepening of response and 6 patients had a maintenance of response with continued treatment. The proposed treatment regimen is further supported by the data with duration of response. Nevertheless, continued evaluation of the long-term efficacy outcomes remains warranted.
L729: For information on the efficacy data in the context of available therapy for patients with relapsed or refractory follicular lymphoma after at least 2 prior therapies, see Section 8.4.
L730: ## 8.2. Review of Safety
L731: ## Data:
L732: The safety database included 447 patients in the safety-evaluable population of Study GO29781 defined as all patients in Group A and Group B who received at least one dose of mosunetuzumab (as a single agent by IV administration). The primary safety analysis set comprised of 218 patients in the Group B expansion cohorts treated at the RP2D/intended registration dose and schedule of 1/2/60/30 mg (referred to as B11 RP2D), and includes 90 patients with R/R FL \ge2 prior therapies (referred to as B11 FL RP2D) and other histology-specific cohorts (88 DLBCL/trFL, 25 MCL, and 14 Richter's transformation; one patient with melanoma who was enrolled in error in the R/R DLBCL/trFL RP2D expansion cohort and included in safety analyses because they received one dose of mosunetuzumab treatment). In addition to the 218 patients treated with the registration dosing regimen, an additional 196 patients treated at other doses in Group B (Cycle 1 step-up) dose escalation and interim expansion cohorts (414 patients in total of mixed NHL histologies; referred as Group B) were included in the safety database to provide a comprehensive overview of the safety profile of single agent mosunetuzumab IV using the Cycle 1 step-up dosing schedule. Safety data from 33 patients with R/R NHL initially treated with escalating doses of mosunetuzumab IV monotherapy administered on a q3w fixed dosing schedule (referred as Group A) were also analyzed to complete the safety evaluation of mosunetuzumab IV monotherapy and to support exposure-response (E-R) modeling analyses and justification of RP2D determination. As the
L733: dosing schedule was different, and patients in Group A received doses of mosunetuzumab per cycle of \le 2.8 mg, the starting dose in the Group B dose-escalation (C1D15 dose in the B1 cohort), Group A was analyzed separately from Group B (see Table 23).
L734: The safety review is of data from Study GO29781 collected up to a clinical cutoff of 27 August 2021, and is an update to the primary analysis based on an earlier CCOD of 15 March 2021.
L735: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L736: The FDA's safety review was primarily focused on the 218 patients treated at the RP2D (1/2/60/30 mg) in Study GO29781, which includes the primary follicular lymphoma population of 90 patients, with a data cutoff of August 27, 2021. The safety data from the additional 196 patients with NHL (Group B) treated at different doses or schedules from the RP2D were considered supportive.
L737: ## 8.2.1. Safety Review Approach
L738: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L739: The focus of the safety review in Study GO29781 was to establish the safety profile of mosunetuzumab in R/R NHL patients, including R/R FL patients who received \ge 2 prior therapies.
L740: The safety profile of single agent mosunetuzumab was assessed by analyzing the incidence and severity of all treatment-emergent AEs, serious AEs (including Grade 5 AEs), adverse events of special interest (AESIs), AEs leading to discontinuation, AEs leading to dose modification (dose reduction or interruption), clinical laboratory test abnormalities (hematology and chemistry), electrocardiograms (ECGs), vital signs, and anti-drug antibodies (ADAs).
L741: Treatment-emergent AEs were defined as AEs with onset on the day of or after first administration of study treatment. After the initiation of the study drug, all AEs with an onset date between the first dose of study drug and 90 days after the last administration of study drug or initiation of another anti-cancer treatment, whichever occurred first, were reported. After this AE reporting period, only SAEs assessed to be related to prior study treatment were reported. Per protocol, all deaths attributed to progression of disease were to be reported as an AE (preferred term malignant neoplasm progression) if the event occurred during the protocol-specified reporting period.
L742: Information on categorization of AEs is provided in Section 8.2.3.
L743: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L744: Safety analyses were conducted on the complete datasets provided by the Applicant with an August 27, 2021 data cutoff. The primary safety population was comprised of the 218 patients
L745: with NHL in the group B dose expansion cohort of study GO29781 who received step-up dosing during cycle 1 of mosunetuzumab monotherapy at the RP2D of 1/2/60/30 mg. This data pool provided an adequate number of patients for a review of safety.
L746: Ninety patients with follicular lymphoma in the B11 expansion cohort were analyzed separately to inform safety in the proposed indication in patients with relapsed or refractory follicular lymphoma.
L747: The clinical review of safety for this BLA is based on the following:
L748: - Clinical study report for study GO29781
- Protocol and statistical analysis plan for study GO29781
- Case report forms and safety narratives
- Summary of clinical safety
- Proposed labeling for mosunetuzumab
L749: The Applicant reported adverse events using single MedDRA preferred terms (PTs). For increased sensitivity, FDA used a combination of grouped PTs, as defined in Appendix 19.5. Adverse events that involve more than one body system were consolidated and reported under the most commonly involved or most appropriate body system. Unless noted, all presented analyses use the FDA grouped PTs.
L750: The Applicant reported treatment emergent deaths due to progressive disease as an adverse event per protocol. For FDA analyses, treatment emergent deaths due to progressive disease are categorized as death due to progressive disease and are not considered an adverse event.
L751: ## Review of the Safety Database
L752: ## Overall Exposure
L753: ## Data:
L754: Information on the safety database and analysis subsets relevant to the evaluation of the safety of single agent mosunetuzumab IV is summarized in the table below.

L759: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L760: Single agent mosunetuzumab administered IV by Cycle 1 step-up dosing was evaluated in 196 patients with R/R NHL the Group B dose escalation at doses ranging from 0.4/1/2.8 mg to 1/2/60 mg (dose administered on C1D1/C1D8/C1D15, C2D1 and subsequent q3w cycles) and 218 patients at the proposed registration dose identified as the RP2D and schedule (1 mg on C1D1, 2 mg on C1D8, 60 mg on C1D15 and C2D1, and 30 mg for C3D1 and subsequent q3w cycles; see Section 6.2.2.1). Mosunetuzumab treatment was administered for a fixed duration, up to a maximum of 8 cycles for patients who achieved a CR after 8 cycles (approximately 5.5 months), or extended to 17 cycles for patients who achieved a PR or maintained stable disease after 8 cycles (approximately one year).
L761: Patients in the B11 RP2D cohort received a median of 8 cycles (range: 1–17) of mosunetuzumab IV monotherapy treatment. Most patients received their planned doses of mosunetuzumab; the median dose intensity was 99.4% and 81.7% of patients achieved a dose intensity of &gt;90%. The median duration of treatment of mosunetuzumab was 4.9 months (range 0.03–13.8), which corresponds approximately to the median of 8 x q3w cycles received. At the time of the CCOD, the median observation time in the B11 RP2D cohort was 14.3 months (range: 0.1–27.9 months).
L762: Cumulative exposure to mosunetuzumab in Group B was less than in the B11 R2PD cohort, due to fewer median number of cycles received and the lower doses used in the dose escalation portion of the study to establish the RP2D. Generally, the extent of exposure to mosunetuzumab in the B11 FL RP2D cohort was similar to the B11 RP2D cohort, with the exception of a higher proportion of patients in the B11 FL RP2D cohort that received the full
L763: 8 cycles (58.9%) of treatment compared to the B11 RP2D cohort (36.7%), due to lower rates of early disease progression in the patients with R/R FL.
L764: Overall, the extent of exposure to mosunetuzumab in Study G029781 is consistent with the expected exposure and treatment duration for the indicated patient population.
L765: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L766: The FDA's safety review was focused on the 218 patients treated at the RP2D in Study G029781 (referred to as the primary safety population). The proposed dosing regimen was mosunetuzumab administered at 1.0 mg on Cycle 1 Day 1 (C1D1), 2.0 mg on C1D8, 60 mg on C1D15 and C2D1, and 30 mg for subsequent doses of a 21-day cycle. The median exposure duration for patients in the B11 RP2D cohort and FL cohort was similar, 4.9 months each. Eighty-one to eighty-two percent of patients achieved a relative dose intensity of &gt;90% in the FL cohort and the B11 RP2D cohort. A larger percentage of patients received 8 cycles of therapy in the B11 FL cohort as compared to the B11 RP2D cohort which likely reflects the heterogeneity of the histologies included in the RP2D cohort, including aggressive NHL histologies. The safety data provided for patients treated beyond cycles 8 is limited for both cohorts.

L769: ## **Relevant characteristics of the safety population:**
L770: ## **Data:**
L771: The demographics of the primary safety population (B11 RP2D cohort, n=218) was similar to the primary efficacy population (B11 FL RP2D, n=90; see Table 19) with respect to gender (66.5% male) and race (82.1% White) while the median age (64.0 years) and proportion of patients &gt;65 years (43.1%) was slightly higher.
L772: Patients with R/R NHL of the following histologies were represented in the B11 RP2D cohort: FL: 41.3%; DLBCL/trFL: 40.4%; MCL: 11.5%; Richter's transformation: 6.4%; and other histologies: 0.5%. A higher proportion of patients in the B11 RP2D cohort compared to the B11 FL RP2D cohort had ECOG PS 1 (54.1% vs. 41.1%), Stage IV disease at study entry (60.8% vs. 48.9%), and had received prior CAR-T therapy (13.8% vs. 3.3%), which may reflect the approval status of this therapeutic option at the time.
L773: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L774: Overall, patients with R/R NHL in the primary safety population had similar demographic and baseline characteristics as patients with R/R FL in the primary efficacy population (and expected target population). The noted differences may be attributed to the more aggressive nature of DLBCL/trFL, which was represented in 40.4% of patients in the B11 RP2D cohort.
L775: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L776: The patient characteristics for patients in the B11 FL cohort and the B11 RP2D cohort are shown in the table below. In the 218 patients in the B11 RP2D cohort, the median age was 64, 67% were male, the majority of patients were White at 82%, and all patients had an ECOG status of 0-1. In the 90 patients in the B11 FL cohort, the median age was younger at age 60, 61% were male, 82% were White, and all patients had an ECOG status of 0-1. The median number of prior therapies was 3 in both cohorts. Of note, 38% of patients in the FL cohort had received 2 prior therapies. Fifty-two percent of patients in the FL cohort had progression of disease within 24 months (POD 24) disease.
L777: Of note, there was limited representation regarding race and ethnicity with 3% to 4% Black or African American and 5% to 8% Hispanic or Latino. In addition, the median age in the FL cohort was 60. Despite this cohort consisting of patients with relapsed or refractory disease, the population was younger than the median age of initial diagnosis in the U.S. at age 63 years (SEER Cancer Statistics 2020).
