L783: ## Adequacy of the safety database:
L784: ## The Applicant's Position:
L785: The Applicant believes that the safety analysis set of 414 patients with R/R NHL including 218 patients treated with the registration dosing regimen (of which 90 patients with R/R FL \ge2 Prior Therapies represented the patient population for the proposed indication) is sufficient to adequately evaluate the incidence of toxicities and to support registration of mosunetuzumab for the proposed indication.
L786: The adequacy of the total patient exposure of mosunetuzumab to support registration in the target population was discussed at a Type B End-of-Phase I meeting (17 March 2020. Ref ID: 4577186). The FDA considered the planned safety database at that time to be reasonable.
L787: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L788: The size of the extended safety database of 414 patients with NHL who received mosunetuzumab monotherapy, the size of the primary safety database of 218 patients with NHL who received mosunetuzumab at the RP2D, and the size of the primary efficacy population of 90 patients with FL are considered adequate for review.
L789: ## 8.2.2. Adequacy of Applicant's Clinical Safety Assessments
L790: ## Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
L791: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L792: No issues relating to safety data integrity or quality were identified for Study GO29781.
L793: There were no reported major COVID-19-related protocol deviations with a potential impact on AE reporting.
L794: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L795: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. The quality of the safety data submitted was adequate for substantive review. The Applicant provided full datasets for patients enrolled in GO29781.
L796: ## **Categorization of Adverse Event**
L797: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L798: Verbatim descriptions for AEs entered by the investigators were assigned to preferred terms (PTs) by using MedDRA Version 24.0. AE severity (with the exception of CRS events) and routine laboratory parameters were graded using the NCI CTCAE v4.0. AEs were then presented by PT and system organ class (SOC).
L799: Dedicated analyses were conducted for adverse events of special interest (AESI) specific for mosunetuzumab based on evolving clinical experience in clinical studies. The AESI categories were cytokine release syndrome (CRS), neurologic AEs and a subset of driving-impacting cognition or consciousness neurologic adverse event (DI-CCNAEs), hematologic AEs, hemophagocytic lymphohistiocytosis (HLH), tumor lysis syndrome, tumor flare, hepatic AEs, infections, and pneumonitis or interstitial lung disease (as requested by the FDA at the Type B Pre-BLA meeting held on 21 January 2021, to discuss content and format. Ref ID: 4749002). Search definitions used were based on either singular MedDRA Preferred Term [PT] or multiple PTs under defined SMQs, SOCs or specific adverse grouped terms as detailed in Table 3 of the GO29781 CSRs.
L800: Per protocol, investigators graded CRS events according to the Lee 2014 grading criteria (Lee et al. 2014). CRS events based on the ASTCT 2019 grading criteria were derived programmatically from the reported data, based on the presence of fever and the presence and management of hypotension or hypoxia as reported in the CRS signs/symptoms eCRF (Lee et al. 2019). CRS events by both Lee 2014 and ASTCT 2019 grading are reported in the clinical study reports for the GO29781 study supporting this application. The summary of safety results presented in Section 8.2.4 below describe CRS events according to ASTCT 2019 grading criteria.
L801: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L802: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. All adverse events and serious adverse events were captured during the trial and for up to 90 days after the last dose of study treatment or until the initiation of another systemic anti-cancer therapy, whichever occurred first. The FDA's
L803: review of safety is focused on treatment emergent adverse events (TEAE) that occurred within 30 days of the last dose of study treatment. Given the single-arm trial design and the absence of a comparator arm, FDA considers all TEAEs regardless of investigator attribution.
L804: ## Routine Clinical Tests
L805: ## The Applicant's Position:
L806: Routine clinical tests included physical examinations, laboratory tests, ECGs and vital signs.
L807: A complete physical exam was scheduled at screening with targeted symptom-directed physical exams schedule at subsequent visits. As part of the clinical examination, patients were routinely assessed for any signs or symptoms of neurologic toxicity.
L808: Laboratory tests for hematology, serum chemistry and creatinine, coagulation parameters, LDH, C-reactive protein and serum ferritin were conducted at local laboratories at the study site. Viral serology and detection was conducted at screening.
L809: TriPLICATE 12-lead electrocardiogram (ECG) recordings were scheduled at screening (baseline), before and at the end of infusion on Days 1 and 15 of Cycle 1 and before the infusion on Cycle 4. ECGs were also performed when clinically indicated.
L810: Vital signs (systolic, and diastolic BP, respiratory rate, pulse oximetry, pulse rate and body temperature) were assessed before, during, at the end, and after infusions. In the event of a suspected anaphylactic reaction during mosunetuzumab administration patients were monitored with continuous electrocardiogram and pulse oximetry.
L811: Specific tests for PK, ADAs, serum cytokines and other biomarkers in blood samples or other biological samples were conducted at a central lab/specialty bioanalytics lab.
L812: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L813: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. The frequency of safety monitoring was considered adequate in the context of the study.

L840: ## The Applicant's Position:
L841: A total of 82 of 218 patients (37.6%) in the B11 RP2D cohort had died. Table 26 summarizes deaths by cause. Progressive disease accounted for the majority of deaths (68/218 patients [31.2%]). A higher proportion of PD-related deaths occurred in patients with DLBCL, MCL, and Richter's transformation than in patients with FL. For this reason, the lowest proportion of deaths occurred in the B11 FL RP2D cohort (8/90 patients [8.9%]), than in the B11 RP2D cohort (82/218 [37.6%] or Group B overall (174/414 [42.0%]).
L842: Of the remaining 14 deaths in the B11 RP2D cohort, 10 deaths were in patients who had discontinued mosunetuzumab treatment and occurred outside of protocol-specified AE reporting period with the primary cause of death as "Other" entered on the Study Completion/Early Discontinuation eCRF page. Specific details provided by the investigator are described in the footnote (f) to Table 26. The other 4 deaths in the B11 RP2D cohort were due to non-PD fatal AEs (i.e., other than malignant neoplasm progression, see below).
L843: Grade 5 AEs were reported during the initial treatment with mosunetuzumab in 32/218 (14.7%) patients in the B11 RP2D cohort. The majority (28/32) of Grade 5 AEs were disease progression (PT of malignant neoplasm progression) occurring during the protocol-specified AE reporting
L844: period. The 4 non-PD Grade 5 AEs were pneumonia, cholangitis, death 'not otherwise specified' (reported as PT of death; patient in B11 FL RP2D cohort), and sepsis (see Table 27).
L845: Two additional non-PD Grade 5 AE of pneumonia and sepsis occurred in Group B at lower mosunetuzumab doses. One other patient (in the Group A fixed-dose cohort) died due to a fatal event of hemophagocytic lymphohistiocytosis (see Table 27).
L846: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L847: Treatment emergent deaths were assessed up to 90 days post treatment discontinuation. For safety, the FDA considers all-cause treatment emergent adverse events in the evaluation versus those indicated as treatment-related by the Applicant. The FDA concurs that as of the August 27, 2021 data cutoff, a total of 82 deaths occurred within the B11 RP2D safety population, with 4 deaths occurring with mosunetuzumab due to an adverse event. The 4 deaths included events of pneumonia, sepsis, cholangitis, and death unknown.
L848: - For the event of death unknown, this represents a 66 year old African American male with follicular lymphoma who experienced ASTCT grade 3 CRS, confusional state (ICANS), and grade 4 acute kidney injury requiring continuous renal replacement therapy by Day 27. The CRS, acute kidney injury, and confusional state all resolved by Day 29. The patient was discharged from the hospital on Day 48, with a follow-up visit on Day 56 demonstrating a normal exam and labs. On Day 60, the patient was reported to have died due to unknown causes.
L849: As noted in the Applicant's position, 28 patients in the B11 RP2D cohort were reported to have died due to malignant progression of disease while on treatment or within 90 days of last dose (Table 27 above). However, one patient, a 74 year old White male with DLBCL developed Grade 1 confusional state on Day 7 and Grade 4 hemophagocytic lymphohistiocytosis (HLH) on Day 8. Day 10 soluble IL2 receptor serum level was 20,000 pg/mL. By Day 13, the patient was reported to show "clinical disease progression, worsening of encephalopathy, and multiorgan dysfunction," which appeared consistent with a clinical presentation of HLH. Mosunetuzumab treatment was discontinued on Day 17 due to disease progression; however, the relative contribution of mosunetuzumab to the development of HLH versus the contribution of progressive disease to the development of HLH remains unclear. For additional information on the evaluation of HLH with mosunetuzumab, see Section 8.2.4 below.
L850: The 90 Day Safety Update provided safety data collected through January 3, 2022. No new fatal AEs were reported in the B11 RP2D cohort in the safety update.

L857: ## The Applicant's Position:
L858: Serious adverse events (SAE) including the Grade 5 AEs, were reported in 114/218 (52.3%) of patients in the B11 RP2D cohort (Table 29). The most frequent SAEs (≥2% of patients) were CRS (20.6% by ASTCT 2019 grade), malignant neoplasm progression (12.8% [all reported as Grade 5 AEs]), pyrexia (4.6%), and pneumonia (3.2%). SAEs in the SOC of Infections and Infestations were reported in 17.0% of patients.
L859: The majority of patients with serious CRS events experienced Grade 1–2 events at the highest grade. Serious pyrexia events were also predominantly Grade 1–2 and did not occur
L860: concurrently with CRS.
L861: Overall, 42/90 patients (46.7%) in the B11 FL RP2D cohort experienced SAEs. Compared to the B11 RP2D cohort there was a similar proportion of patients with serious CRS events (21 patients [23.3%] by ASTCT 2019) and a smaller proportion of patients with malignant neoplasm progression (1 patient [1.1%]). The frequency and nature of most commonly reported SAEs in the B11 RP2D cohort were similar to overall Group B.
L862: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L863: In general, the FDA agrees with the Applicant's summary of all-cause serious treatment emergent adverse events. The table below provides a summary of the FDA analysis of SAEs with an incidence of 2% or greater using grouped preferred terms in the B11 RP2D NHL and B11 FL pools. In the B11 RP2D cohort, 52% (114/218) experienced an SAE, most often due to CRS (21%), pneumonia (5%), pyrexia (5%), sepsis (3%), and a 2% incidence each of tumor flare, urinary tract infection, pleural effusion, and renal insufficiency.

L879: ## The Applicant's Position:
L880: Adverse events led to the discontinuation of mosunetuzumab treatment in 9/218 patients (4.1%) in the B11 RP2D cohort (Table 32). In 7 patients (3.2%) the AEs were serious and were fatal events of sepsis and cholangitis (see Table 27), Grade 4 Epstein-Barr viremia, Grade 3 CRS (ASTCT; Grade 4 by Lee 2014), subdural hematoma and myocardial infarction, and Grade 2 CRS. At the RP2D, the two serious CRS events, were the only AEs that led to mosunetuzumab discontinuation in more than one patient (both in the B11 FL RP2D cohort) and both events resolved. The CRS, sepsis, and myocardial infarction events which led to treatment discontinuation were considered to be related to mosunetuzumab by the investigator.
L881: A similar proportion of patients in the B11 FL RP2D cohort (4.4%) and Group B (4.6%) discontinued mosunetuzumab due to AEs compared to the B11 RP2D cohort.
L882: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L883: In general, the FDA agrees with the Applicant's summary of all-cause adverse events leading to treatment discontinuation. For safety, the FDA considers all-cause treatment emergent adverse events in the evaluation versus those indicated as treatment-related by the Applicant. The FDA analysis of TEAEs in the B11 RP2D leading to treatment discontinuation is consistent with the data presented in Table 32 above. Overall, treatment discontinuation due to a TEAE occurred in 4% (9/218). The most common adverse event leading to discontinuation was CRS.
L884: ## **Dose Interruption/Reduction Due to Adverse Effects**
L885: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L886: Adverse events led to the mosunetuzumab dose being interrupted (delayed) or modified (reduced) in 73/218 of patients (33.5%) in the B11 RP2D cohort. The most frequently reported events (\ge 2\% of patients) were neutropenia/neutrophil count decreased (25 patients [11.5%]; predominantly Grade 3 or 4), and CRS (17 patients [7.8%] by ASTCT 2019 grade; predominantly Grade 1 or 2). Dose interruptions due to AEs were the most frequent action taken with mosunetuzumab and in most cases the event resolved and patients were able to continue mosunetuzumab treatment.
L887: Per protocol, dose reductions in mosunetuzumab were permitted if it was determined by the investigator that the clinical benefit may be maintained. A reduced dose was to be considered for the next dose when resuming dosing following the occurrence and resolution of Grade 3 CRS, other study-treatment-related Grade 3 toxicity, serious adverse event, or a DLT. Reductions in mosunetuzumab dose were infrequent, occurring in 7 patients (3.2%) in the B11 RP2D cohort and most frequently resulted from AEs with onset in Cycle 1. The AEs that led to dose reductions in more than one patient were erythematous rash/rash and Grade \ge 3 CRS.
L888: The overall frequency and the most commonly reported AEs leading to dose interruption or modification of mosunetuzumab were similar in the B11 RP2D cohort, B11 FL RP2D cohort, and overall Group B.
L889: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L890: In general, the FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. The table below provides a summary of the FDA analysis of adverse events leading to a dose interruption. In the B11 RP2D cohort of 218 patients, 33% experienced adverse events leading to dose interruption, most often due to neutropenia, infection, and CRS.
L891: For dose reductions, as noted by the Applicant, 3% (7/218) of patients in the B11 RP2D cohort experienced an adverse event leading to dose reduction, most often due to CRS and rash.

L911: ## The Applicant's Position:
L912: The majority of patients treated with mosunetuzumab IV monotherapy by Cycle 1 step-up dosing at the intended registration dose and schedule experienced at least one AE of any grade (see Table 34). Most AEs were Grade 1–2 in severity. The most common AE reported was CRS (39.4% by ASTCT 2019 grade), which were predominantly Grade 1–2, limited primarily to Cycle 1, and were manageable and reversible (all CRS events resolved). Other common AEs (\ge 20% incidence) in the B11 RP2D cohort were fatigue, neutropenia/neutrophil count decreased, pyrexia, hypophosphatemia, and headache.
L913: By SOC, AEs were most frequently reported (\ge50% patients) under General Disorders and Administration Site Conditions (61.5%) driven mainly by fatigue and pyrexia, Skin and Subcutaneous Tissue Disorders (56.4%) driven mainly by rash and pruritus, Gastrointestinal Disorders (55.0%) driven mainly by diarrhea, constipation, and nausea, and Metabolism and Nutrition Disorders (52.3%) driven mainly by hypophosphatemia, hypokalemia and hypomagnesemia.
L914: Overall, the nature, severity, and frequency of the most common AEs in the B11 RP2D cohort were similar to those in B11 FL RP2D cohort, and overall Group B.
L915: Grade 3-4 AEs were reported in 66.5% of patients in the Group B11 RP2D cohort (Table 34). The most frequently reported Grade 3–4 AEs (\ge5% in the B11 RP2D cohort) were neutropenia/neutrophil count decreased (24.3%), hypophosphatemia (14.7%), anemia/hemoglobin decreased (8.3%), thrombocytopenia/platelet count decreased (6.9%), and hyperglycemia (5.5%). The majority of Grade 3-4 AEs were manageable with dose modification or interruption of mosunetuzumab.
L916: Based on the single-arm design of the GO29781 study, for the proposed label, ADRs from mosunetuzumab IV monotherapy treatment were identified based on review of AEs (PTs or grouped PTs) with incidence \ge10% (all grades) or \ge5% for Grade 3-4 based on the B11 RP2D population (N=218). Events with an incidence of &lt;10% (all grades) or &lt;5% (Grade 3-4) could be included if there was a medical rationale supporting the assessment (such as a known class effect/known effect from mechanism of action), and at least one of the observed reports was
L917: assessed by the Applicant as related to mosunetuzumab. Medical judgment would be further applied to adjudicate potential ADRs, taking into consideration the extent to which the AE was consistent with the pharmacology of the drug, apparent dose response trend, re-challenge response, temporality and the consistency of the pattern of symptoms across studies/indications, confounders and quality of information. AEs that met these criteria are the basis for the adverse reactions summarized in Table 36.
L918: **Table 36: Applicant – Adverse Reactions**

L923: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L924: Treatment emergent adverse events were assessed from the start of study drug until 30 days after the last study drug administration. In the B11 RP2D cohort, any grade TEAEs were reported in 98% and Grade 3 or 4 adverse events occurred in 66%. The FDA analysis of TEAEs (&gt;5%) using FDA grouped preferred terms (Section 20.5) is displayed in the table below in decreasing order of incidence by system organ class and preferred term. The most common TEAEs (excluding laboratory abnormalities) in the B11 RP2D cohort (≥20%) were cytokine release syndrome, fatigue, rash, pyrexia, headache, cough and edema. The most common Grade 3 or 4 adverse events (≥3%) were pneumonia, cytokine release syndrome, sepsis, and renal insufficiency. For laboratory abnormalities, see the Laboratory Findings section below.
L925: For adverse reactions, the FDA disagrees with the Applicant's definition as stated above. Given the single-arm trial design of GO29781, FDA considers all TEAEs as an adverse drug reaction unless unequivocally due to the underlying disease or an extraneous cause. For the mosunetuzumab prescribing information (USPI), the adverse reactions included are based on the FDA definition.

L941: ## The Applicant's Position:
L942: Among the patients in the B11 RP2D cohort, the most frequent treatment-emergent Grade \ge 3 worsening laboratory parameter shifts (\ge10% of patients) were decreases in lymphocytes (the pharmacodynamic action of mosunetuzumab to deplete B-cells), neutrophils, leukocytes, hemoglobin, and platelets for hematology laboratory parameters, and decreases in phosphate, increases in glucose and urate for chemistry laboratory parameters. These laboratory abnormalities are consistent with neutropenia/neutrophil count decreased, anemia, thrombocytopenia/platelet count decreased, hypophosphatemia, and hyperglycemia being the most frequently reported Grade 3-4 AEs in the B11 RP2D cohort (see Section 6.14.1.2.2 and 6.14.2.2.2 of CSR).
L943: One patient in B11 RP2D, and 10 patients in Group B overall, were identified as potential Hy's law cases due to laboratory results of &gt;3\timesULN for AST or ALT and/or &gt;2\timesULN for total bilirubin. These liver enzyme abnormalities occurred in patients who later experienced disease progression or occurred concurrently with CRS or as symptoms of CRS (see Section 6.14.2.2.1 of CSR).
L944: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L945: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position and the summary of laboratory-shift results for the B11 RP2D cohort. Cytopenias were common during treatment with mosunetuzumab. The evaluation of laboratory data indicates that the frequency of all grade cytopenias and Grade 3-4 cytopenias were higher than those reported as a TEAEs. Based on the laboratory data in the B11 RP2D cohort, Grade 3-4 neutropenia, anemia, and thrombocytopenia occurred in 38%, 19%, and 12% of patients, respectively. Additionally, Grade 4 neutropenia occurred in 19% and Grade 4 thrombocytopenia occurred in 5% of patients. Based on the incidence and severity of cytopenias, FDA has included a Warning and Precaution for cytopenias in the USPI. The table below provides the rates of treatment emergent laboratory abnormalities in the B11 FL cohort per laboratory shift evaluation.

L950: ## Vital Signs
L951: ## Data:
L952: No clinically relevant effects of mosunetuzumab on absolute or change from baseline values for diastolic or systolic blood pressure, heart rate, respiratory rate, temperature, weight, and oxygen saturation were observed across the safety population as a whole.
L953: The changes in vital signs for individual patients in the B11 RP2D cohort, B11 FL RP2D cohort, and Group B were consistent with the reported AEs.
L954: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L955: The FDA agrees that the observed changes in vital signs that are clinically relevant are in the context of patients experiencing adverse events. The most relevant vital sign changes were in the setting of CRS following mosunetuzumab administration and included pyrexia, hypoxia, and hypotension. Of 86 patients in the B11 RP2D cohort that experienced CRS per ASTCT criteria, pyrexia, hypoxia, and hypotension occurred in 100%, 23%, and 36%, respectively.
L956: ## Electrocardiograms (ECGs)
L957: ## **Data:**
L958: Across the safety population as a whole, there were no clinically relevant effects of mosunetuzumab on heart rate, PR duration, QRS duration, QT duration, QTcB, QTcF, and RR duration, as assessed by absolute or change from baseline values at scheduled ECG assessments during the study.
L959: The majority of ECG abnormalities were considered to be not clinically significant by the investigator. Of 11 patients in Groups A and B who had clinically significant post-baseline ECG abnormalities, 4 patients had ECG abnormalities at baseline.
L960: Adverse events in the SOC of Cardiac Disorders were reported in 10.3% of patients (Group A and B); most were Grade 1 or 2. Grade 3 events were reported in 4 patients, all in Group B (atrial flutter, atrioventricular block second degree, and myocardial infarction (all serious), and atrial fibrillation). In the 11 patients who had clinically significant post-baseline ECG abnormalities, cardiac-related AEs were reported in 5 patients, and all were Grade 1 or 2.
L961: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L962: Based on the available data, there were no clinically relevant effects of mosunetuzumab on cardiac function and cardiac disorders are not considered a risk for mosunetuzumab.
L963: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L964: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position and has provided additional information. Triplicate ECG recordings were required at baseline, pre and post infusion on C1D1 and C1D15 and preinfusion on C4D1. Based on FDA grouping of related preferred terms in the B11 RP2D cohort, ECG-related TEAEs in the SOC cardiac disorders included sinus tachycardia (4%), tachycardia (2%), atrial fibrillation (2%), arrhythmia (&lt;1%), bradycardia (&lt;1%), and sinus bradycardia (&lt;1%). In the FL cohort, ECG-related TEAEs in the SOC cardiac disorders included sinus tachycardia (3%), tachycardia (2%), and bradycardia (1%).
L965: ## **QT Assessment**
L966: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L967: As a monoclonal antibody, mosunetuzumab is not expected to interact directly with the human ether-à-go-go-related gene (hERG) channel and the risk for QT prolongation following mosunetuzumab treatment is considered to be low. Clinical data indicate that mosunetuzumab does not have a clinically relevant effect on QTcF interval across a wide dose range (0.05 to 60 mg) (see Electrocardiograms (ECGs) above). No dedicated QT study was conducted. The QT assessment plan was discussed at the Type B Pre-BLA content and format meeting (21 January 2021. Ref ID: 4749002) where the Agency agreed with the Applicant's proposal to not conduct a dedicated QT study and that a separate QT evaluation report was not necessary.
L968: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L969: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. A dedicated QT assessment was not required and was not conducted as per ICH E14 Q&amp;A (R3). However, 2 patients (&lt;1%, Grade 1 and 2, one patient each) developed "electrocardiogram QT prolongation" in the B11 RP2D cohort, both less than 500 ms.
L970: ## Immunogenicity
L971: ## **Data:**
L972: As of the CCOD used for the ADA analyses (4 December 2020), no anti-drug antibodies (ADAs) were detected in any patient at any assessment in the 418 ADA-evaluable patients who received mosunetuzumab IV monotherapy in Study GO29781.
L973: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L974: Based on the available data, the impact of immunogenicity on safety is low.
L975: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L976: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L977: ## 8.2.4. Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues
L978: ## 8.2.4.1 CRS
L979: ## **Data:**
L980: In the B11 RP2D cohort, CRS events by ASTCT 2019 grade were reported in 39.4% of patients. The majority were Grade 1–2 severity (95.1% [117/123 CRS events]). Grade 3 CRS events were reported in 5 patients (2.3%) and Grade 4 CRS event was reported in a single patient (0.5%) diagnosed with FL in leukemic phase. Serious CRS events were reported in 20.6% patients in the B11 RP2D cohort (see Table 29); the majority were Grade 1–2 at maximum severity and considered serious predominantly due to the requirement for hospitalization or prolonged hospitalization.
L981: CRS events occurred predominantly in Cycle 1 and were mainly associated with Day 1 and Day 15 dose administrations, with the highest frequency of CRS events driven by Grade 1 and 2 events following the C1D15 dose in B11 RP2D. The frequency of CRS events decreased in the subsequent treatment cycles. The 6 Grade 3-4 CRS events, all of which were serious, followed C1D15 and C2D1 infusions. The median duration of CRS events was 3 days (range: 1-29 days)
L982: Most CRS events were managed with supportive care (i.e., symptomatic management of constitutional symptoms), with or without anti-IL-6/corticosteroid therapy. CRS events requiring dose interruption/modification were reported in 17 patients (7.8%) in the B11 RP2D cohort while CRS events that led to mosunetuzumab treatment discontinuation were reported in 2 patients (0.9%; see Table 32). All CRS events resolved.
L983: The most common treatments administered for any grade CRS were fluids (for hypotension), steroids, tocilizumab, and low flow oxygen (for hypoxia). By ASTST grade:
L984: - Of 80 Grade 1 ASTCT CRS events reported in 61 patients in the B11 RP2D cohort, most events (66/80 [82.5%]) were not treated with tocilizumab, corticosteroids, fluids, vasopressors, or oxygen. Tocilizumab was administered for 7 Grade 1 CRS events (8.8%). Steroids were administered concurrently with 9 Grade 1 CRS events (11.3%), of which 2 of the events (2.5%) also included tocilizumab administration.
- Of 37 Grade 2 ASTCT CRS events reported in 33 patients in the B11 RP2D cohort, 25 events (67.6%) were treated with fluids, and 18 events (48.6%) were treated with low flow oxygen. Tocilizumab was administered for 10 Grade 2 CRS events (27.0%). Steroids were administered concurrently with 14 Grade 2 CRS events (37.8%), of which 4 of the events (10.8%) were treated with both steroids and tocilizumab.
- Of 5 Grade 3 ASTCT CRS events in 5 patients, 4 events were treated with a single vasopressor, and 2 events were treated with high-flow oxygen (the other 3 events were treated with low flow oxygen). Tocilizumab was administered for 4 of the 5 Grade 3 CRS events (80%), which included all 4 events treated with single vasopressor. Steroids were administered concurrently with 4 Grade 3 CRS events (80%), of which 3 of the events were treated with both steroids and tocilizumab.
- A single Grade 4 ASTCT CRS event required treatment with multiple vasopressors. The patient also received tocilizumab and concurrent steroids.
L985: The overall frequency and severity of CRS events between the B11 RP2D cohort, B11 FL RP2D cohort, and overall Group B were generally similar. One difference noted was the frequency of CRS events following the C1D15 mosunetuzumab infusion, which was slightly higher in the B11 RP2D cohort (when the 60 mg dose was administered; 33.3% by ASTCT 2019 grade) compared to overall Group B (24.4% by ASTCT 2019 grade), driven mainly by a slightly higher incidence of serious CRS events of Grade \ge 2 severity.
L986: ## The Applicant's Position:
L987: Consistent with the mechanism of action of mosunetuzumab, CRS is a serious safety concern that may result in life-threatening consequences if not managed appropriately. To mitigate the risk of CRS, comprehensive product labelling is proposed (see table of adverse reactions, Warnings and Precautions and Dosage and Administration sections of the prescribing information), as well as [REDACTED] (b) (4) enhanced patient labeling in the form of a voluntary non-REMS program Medication Guide (as per 21 CFR 208) [REDACTED] (b) (4). The primary aims of these materials are to educate
L988: healthcare providers (HCPs) on early recognition and management of CRS, and patients on early recognition and presentation to healthcare facilities.
L989: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L990: CRS events were reported and graded according to the Lee 2014 grading criteria. CRS events were also summarized by the ASTCT 2019 grading criteria. The FDA's review is focused on CRS events per ASTCT criteria.
L991: All patients received prophylaxis for CRS with corticosteroid premedication one hour prior to each mosunetuzumab dose during Cycle 1 and 2 (i.e., dexamethasone 20 mg intravenously or methylprednisolone 80 mg administered intravenously). Corticosteroid premedication became optional for Cycle 3 and beyond unless the patient experienced a CRS event. Oral acetaminophen or paracetamol and/or diphenhydramine were administered per institutional standard.
L992: ## Hospitalization Requirements
L993: Administration of mosunetuzumab occurred in a clinical setting with immediate access to intensive care, in addition to neurology and nephrology consultation services on Study GO29781. For the dose escalation portion of Study GO29781, patients received inpatient monitoring through 72 hours after any mosunetuzumab dose that exceeded a previously tested dose level. Following the dose escalation portion, hospitalization was no longer required unless clinically indicated at the study investigator's discretion and in consultation with the Medical Monitor. Patients were, however, observed for at least 90 minutes after completion of each infusion. Therefore, the data from the B11 RP2D cohort represent IV administration of mosunetuzumab in an outpatient healthcare setting with post-infusion monitoring and access to intensive care. Of note, per protocol version 12, for patients that experience Grade 1 or 2 CRS, hospitalization was to be considered for the next dose of mosunetuzumab. For patients that experienced Grade 3 CRS and were able to receive subsequent treatment, hospitalization was mandatory for the next dose.
L994: ## CRS Incidence and Severity
L995: In the B11 RP2D cohort per ASTCT criteria, 39% of patients (86/218) experienced a CRS event, with 21% (45/218) considered serious, primarily due to the need for hospitalization. Of the 86 patients, the most frequently reported CRS signs and symptoms included pyrexia (100%), chills (36%), hypotension (35%), tachycardia (24%), hypoxia (22%), and headache (16%). The majority of patients experienced Grade 1 or Grade 2 CRS at 28% (61/218) and 15% (33/218), respectively. Two percent of patients (5/218) experienced grade 3 CRS with only one patient (&lt;1%) experiencing grade 4 CRS. Most patients (33%) experienced CRS after the Cycle 1, Day 15 dose. The median duration of CRS was 3 days (range: 1 to 29 days), with 75% of events lasting 5 days or less. Recurrent CRS occurred in 11% of patients.
L996: The incidence of CRS by dose was Cycle 1 Day 1 at 15% (33/218), Cycle 1 Day 8 at 5% (10/207),
L997: Cycle 1 Day 15 at 33% (67/201), Cycle 2 Day 1 at 4% (9/198), and Cycle 3 Day 1 at 1% (2/167). The Grade 3 CRS events occurred on Cycle 1 Day 15 and Cycle 2 Day 1. The one Grade 4 CRS event occurred on Cycle 1 Day 15. The time to onset was shortest with the first dose of mosunetuzumab and progressively increased with subsequent doses. The median time to onset on Cycle 1 Day 1 was 5 hours (range: 1 to 73 hours) and Cycle 1 Day 15 was 25 hours (range: 0.1 to 391 hours). See the table below for an overview of CRS in the FL cohort and the B11 RP2D cohort.
L998: Neurologic adverse events occurring concurrently with CRS were observed in 16% (14/86) of patients in the B11 RP2D cohort. The most common neurologic manifestations included headache, confusional state, and anxiety. Additionally, increased liver function tests occurring concurrently with CRS was observed in 3% (3/86) of patients, primarily as AST/ALT increased.
L999: The incidence of CRS per ASTCT in the B11 FL cohort was slightly higher (44%) than in the B11 RP2D cohort, with 23% of events being serious CRS events. The pattern of most frequently reported signs and symptoms of CRS was similar to the B11 RP2D cohort except for a higher incidence of headache (28%). A greater proportion of patients experienced CRS events with Cycle 1 Day 1 (23%, 21/90) than was observed in the B11 RP2D cohort at 15%. The frequency of CRS events with Cycle 1 Day 15 was similar in both cohorts (36% and 33%, respectively). Recurrent CRS occurred in 21% of patients (19/90) in the B11 FL cohort. Concurrent neurologic adverse events occurred in 10% of patients and concurrent increased liver function tests were observed in 2% in the B11 FL cohort.
L1000: ## Management of CRS
L1001: Per the protocol, management of CRS included interruption of mosunetuzumab (as applicable), supportive therapy, anti-IL-6 or corticosteroid therapy, and considerations for the next dose of mosunetuzumab. Tocilizumab and corticosteroids could be considered for prolonged Grade 1 CRS (&gt;2 days) in patients with significant symptoms and/or comorbidities per investigator discretion. For patients with Grade 2 CRS, tocilizumab was to be considered followed by corticosteroids if refractory hypotension after 1-2 doses of tocilizumab. Administration of tocilizumab and corticosteroids were prespecified for Grade 3 and Grade 4 CRS.
L1002: The management of CRS in patients in the B11 FL cohort and B11 RP2D cohort was similar except for the use of tocilizumab without corticosteroids, which was used in 18% of patients with CRS in the B11 FL cohort and 24% of patients with CRS in the B11 RP2D cohort (Table 41 and Table 42). The use of corticosteroids with or without tocilizumab, IV fluids, pressors, oxygen, and intensive care were consistent across the populations. Since the use of tocilizumab and/or corticosteroids were not prespecified for Grade 1 or Grade 2 CRS, there was heterogeneity in the patient cases that received tocilizumab versus those who did not receive tocilizumab, precluding any meaningful interpretation of the data. Further, all cases of CRS resolved and the outcomes in patients treated with supportive therapy, corticosteroids, tocilizumab, or the combination of corticosteroids and tocilizumab were descriptively similar.
L1003: Therefore, the use of tocilizumab and corticosteroids for the management of mosunetuzumab-induced CRS, especially Grade 1 and 2 CRS, remains uncertain and further prospective evaluation is warranted.

L1011: For additional analyses and information on CRS with mosunetuzumab, see Section 6.2.2.1.
L1012: ## Risk Mitigation
L1013: Based on the CRS profile with mosunetuzumab, a box warning for CRS is warranted along with a corresponding medication guide. This is based on the occurrence of serious and life-threatening cases of CRS observed and the need for providers to be accurately aware of the risk. Additionally, the USPI includes the statement that mosunetuzumab should only be administered by a qualified healthcare professional with appropriate medical support to manage severe reactions such as cytokine release syndrome. The management of CRS in relation to mosunetuzumab will be included in the USPI.
L1014: (b) (4)
L1015: ## 8.2.4.2 Infections
L1016: ## Data:
L1017: Infection AEs (broadly defined under the SOC of Infections and Infestations) were reported in 46.8% patients in the B11 RP2D cohort. The majority of infections were Grade 1–2 severity. Grade \ge 3 infections were reported in 15.1% of patients. The most frequently reported Grade 3 event (in \ge 2\% of patients) was pneumonia (5 patients [2.3%]). Grade 4 events were sepsis (3 patients [1.4%]) and Epstein-Barr viremia and septic shock (1 patient [0.5%] each). Grade 5 infections of pneumonia and sepsis were reported in 1 patient (0.5%) each (see Table 27). Serious infections were reported in 37 patients (17.0%), with pneumonia (7 patients [3.2%]) being the most frequent.
L1018: The majority of serious infections did not appear to occur in the context of neutropenia. In the B11 RP2D cohort, four serious infections, enterocolitis bacterial (Grade 3), herpes zoster
L1019: (Grade 2), and two events of sepsis (Grade 5 and Grade 4) occurred concurrently with neutropenia/neutrophil count decreased events.
L1020: At the CCOD, 76 patients (34.9%) had all events resolved and the remaining 26 patients (11.9%) had at least one unresolved or ongoing event.
L1021: The frequencies of infection events and serious events were similar between the B11 RP2D and the B11 FL RP2D cohorts, and Group B overall.
L1022: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1023: Serious infections are anticipated with mosunetuzumab administration due to its mode of action resulting in B-cell depletion. Based on the increased risk of infections associated with mosunetuzumab, serious infections are included in the Warnings and Precautions section of the product labelling with guidance not to administer mosunetuzumab in the presence of active infections and to consider prophylactic antibacterial, antiviral and/or antifungal medications, as appropriate. Certain infections (e.g., upper respiratory tract infections, urinary tract infection, and pneumonia) are included as clinically important adverse reactions in the prescribing information.
L1024: ## FDA Assessment:
L1025: In general, the FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. The FDA agrees with the reported 47% incidence of infections in the B11 RP2D cohort (N = 218) of which 17% were serious infections, including pneumonia in 5% of patients, sepsis in 3%, and urinary tract infection and upper respiratory infection in 2% each. The 14% incidence of Grade 3 or 4 infections in the B11 RP2D cohort was similarly driven by pneumonia (3%), sepsis (3%), and upper respiratory infections (2%). Two patients in the B11 RP2D cohort died due to pneumonia and sepsis, respectively. The incidence of infections in the B11 FL cohort (N = 90) at 51% (46/90) was slightly higher than that observed in the B11 RP2D cohort. Twenty percent (18/90) of patients experienced a serious infection, including urinary tract infections in 4% of patients, sepsis in 3% of patients, and COVID-19, Epstein-Barr viremia, and pneumonia in 2% of patients each. The incidence of Grade 3 or 4 infections was 17% (15/90), most often due to pneumonia (3%), sepsis (3%), COVID-19 (2%), and upper respiratory infections (2%). There were no fatal infections observed in the FL cohort. Infections remained unresolved in 10% of patients (9/90) in the B11 FL cohort at the time of the data cutoff.
L1026: Based on the risk of serious, severe, and fatal infections associated with mosunetuzumab, the FDA agrees with inclusion of a Warning and Precaution for infections in the USPI.
L1027: ## 8.2.4.3 Tumor Flare
L1028: ## Data:
L1029: Tumor flare events were identified by PT of tumor flare, as suspected tumor flare events, or by AESI Grade \ge 2 tumor flare/tumor inflammation specific for mosunetuzumab.
L1030: A total of 9/218 patients (4.1%) in the B11 RP2D cohort experienced events that met the definition of tumor flare events following initial treatment with mosunetuzumab.
L1031: The reported tumor flare events included tumor flare and pleural effusion (4 patients [1.8%] each), and tumor inflammation (1 patient [0.5%]). All reported events were of Grade 2 or Grade 3 maximum severity. Grade 3 events were tumor flare (3 patients [1.4%]) and pleural effusion (2 patients [0.9%]). Serious tumor flare events were reported in 4 patients (1.9%). The median time to onset of first tumor flare events was 13 days (range: 5–84 days), with median duration of 10 days (range: 1–77 days). At the CCOD, the majority of the events (8 of 9 events [88.9%]) had resolved.
L1032: Similar frequencies of tumor flare events and serious tumor flare events were noted between the B11 RP2D and the B11 FL RP2D cohorts and Group B overall. Further details for tumor flare events are provided in the SCS, Section 2.7.6.
L1033: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1034: Consistent with the mechanism of action, tumor flare is likely due to the rapid influx of T cells to tumor sites and can result in enlargement and mass effect compromising surrounding structures. Since there is the potential for clinically significant impact based on compromise of anatomical site of lesions secondary to mass effect, tumor flare is listed as a clinically important adverse reaction and included in the Warnings and Precautions section of the product labelling with monitoring and management advice to allow prescribers to plan and manage patients appropriately.
L1035: ## FDA Assessment:
L1036: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position and the data presented above. The incidence of grade 2 and greater tumor flare in the B11 RP2D cohort and B11 FL cohort were 4% (9/218) and 3% (3/90), respectively. Only one tumor flare adverse event in the RP2D B11 cohort had not resolved by the clinical cutoff date.
L1037: The FDA agrees with the Applicant that the risk of tumor flare and the potential for airway compromise or organ impairment secondary to mass effect is clinically important. Because of the risk, the FDA agrees with inclusion of tumor flare as a Warning and Precaution for mosunetuzumab in the USPI.
L1038: ## 8.2.4.4 Tumor Lysis Syndrome
L1039: ## Data:
L1040: Two TLS events were reported in 2 patients in the B11 RP2D cohort. Both events (a Grade 4 event in a patient with R/R FL and a Grade 3 event in a patient with R/R MCL) were assessed as serious and were preceded by a CRS event on the prior study day. Both patients had received allopurinol prophylaxis. Both TLS events resolved, with no change in mosunetuzumab dose.
L1041: No additional TLS events were reported in other cohorts in Group B. Further details for TLS events are provided in Section 2.7.5 of the SCS.
L1042: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1043: TLS is an oncological emergency when it occurs, however it is well understood by HCPs in the hematology treatment landscape.
L1044: (b) (4)
L1045: (b) (4)
L1046: ## **FDA Assessment:**
L1047: (b) (4)
L1048: The FDA agrees with the data presented above.
L1049: (b) (4)
L1050: In addition, as noted by the Applicant, tumor lysis syndrome is well understood by healthcare providers in the treatment of patients with hematologic malignancies.
L1051: ## 8.2.4.5 Hemophagocytic Lymphohistiocytosis
L1052: ## **FDA Assessment:**
L1053: Hemophagocytic lymphohistiocytosis was identified as an adverse event of special interest by the Applicant for mosunetuzumab and was specifically monitored and evaluated in Study GO29781. There were 4 identified cases of HLH in the overall safety population. One case (0.4%, 1/218) occurred in the B11 RP2D cohort and the 3 other cases occurred in patients receiving different doses or regimens of mosunetuzumab monotherapy. A brief summary of the cases is provided below.
L1054: - B11 RP2D Cohort (1/2/60/30 mg): A 74 year old White male with diffuse large B-cell lymphoma who received the mosunetuzumab step up doses of 1 mg on Cycle 1 Day 1 and 2mg on Cycle 1 Day 8. The patients developed Grade 4 hemophagocytic lymphohistiocytosis (HLH) on Day 8. Day 10 soluble IL2 receptor serum level was 20,000 pg/mL. By Day 13, the patient was reported to show "clinical disease progression, worsening of encephalopathy, and multiorgan dysfunction," which appeared consistent with a clinical presentation of HLH. Mosunetuzumab treatment was discontinued due to disease progression; however, the relative contribution of mosunetuzumab to the development of HLH versus the contribution of progressive disease to the development
L1055: of HLH remains unclear. The patient died due to progressive disease along with ongoing HLH.
L1056: - Dose Escalation Group A (2.8 mg every 3 weeks): A 74 year old Asian female with Richter's transformation received mosunetuzumab through Cycle 2 Day 1 (2 doses). The patient developed Grade 2 HLH on study Day 58 based on high ferritin and low fibrinogen along with a constellation of signs and symptoms that started on study Day 15. The patient developed septic shock, electrolyte imbalance, and seizure as part of HLH. With the evaluation for HLH, a prior marrow aspirate and lymph node biopsy showed concurrent EBV infection, which possibly contributed to the development of HLH. The patient ultimately died due to HLH on study Day 60.
- Dose Escalation Group B (20 mg every 3 weeks, step-up doses): A 67 year old White male with Richter's transformation received mosunetuzumab for the complete treatment regimen of 17 cycles. Approximately 46 days following the last dose of mosunetuzumab, the patient developed CMV and EBV infections and a bone marrow biopsy was consistent with HLH, along with correlative laboratory findings such as high ferritin. The patient was treated with etoposide and dexamethasone for HLH. The patient's HLH was resolving with treatment, however, the patient died due to renal failure around 9 months following his last dose of mosunetuzumab.
- Dose escalation Group B (6 mg every 3 weeks, step-up doses): A 72 year old White male with small lymphocytic lymphoma (SLL) received mosunetuzumab through Cycle 1 Day 15 (3 doses). The patient died due to progressive disease on study Day 26. Upon autopsy, the cause of death was multi-organ failure due to HLH and chronic active EBV infection with progressive SLL. The relationship with EBV infection and HLH in the setting of progressive disease remains unclear.
L1057: The cases of HLH raise concern regarding an association of HLH with mosunetuzumab, however the relationship remains unclear at this time. Further, the contribution of EBV viremia also remains unclear. However, based on the mechanism of action of mosunetuzumab, there is a potential risk for potentiation of infections such as EBV and CMV and the development of HLH. Continued prospective evaluation of HLH in ongoing trials evaluating mosunetuzumab is warranted to further characterize the risk of HLH with mosunetuzumab. Additionally, a PMR to evaluate safety with longer term use of mosunetuzumab will be issued and HLH will be included in the assessment.
L1058: ## 8.2.4.6 Neurologic toxicity
L1059: ## FDA Assessment:
L1060: Neurologic toxicity was identified as an adverse event of special interest by the Applicant for mosunetuzumab and monitored and evaluated in Study GO29791. On Study GO29781, all patients were required to undergo a baseline complete neurologic exam prior to the first administration of mosunetuzumab. If new or worsening neurologic toxicity occurred, it was
L1061: recommended that the patient be referred to a neurologist and corticosteroids would potentially be considered for treatment. Imaging was performed as clinically indicated. Per the protocol, neurologic adverse events (NAE) were defined as adverse events that mapped to either the system organ class (SOC) Nervous System Disorders or Psychiatric disorders. Based on this, 56% of patients in the B11 RP2D (N = 218) cohort experienced at least one neurologic adverse event as compared to 69% of patients in the B11 FL cohort (N = 90) as shown in Table 43 below. The majority of NAEs were Grade 1 and 2 in both cohorts, with 5% of events being Grade 3 in the B11 RP2D cohort and 4% being Grade 3 in the B11 FL cohort. Serious NAEs were observed in 6% in the B11 RP2D cohort and 4% in the B11 FL cohort. Twenty-one percent of events in both cohorts were unresolved at the time of data cutoff (August 2021).
L1062: The Applicant also identified a subset of NAEs with preferred terms that could potentially impact cognition, consciousness, and the ability to drive, i.e., driving-impacting cognition or consciousness neurologic adverse event (DI-CCNAE), including but not limited to: amnesia, aphasia, confusional state, delirium, depressed level of consciousness, disturbance in attention, encephalopathy, hallucination, hepatic encephalopathy, insomnia, memory impairment, seizure, visual hallucination, and vertigo. Using this definition, 12% of patients in the B11 RP2D cohort and 10% of patients in the B11 FL cohort experienced DI-CCNAEs, respectively. The majority were Grade 1 or 2, with 3% being Grade 3 in the B11 RP2D cohort. The most common events in the B11 RP2D cohort were confusional state (4%), fatigue (2%), and neurotoxicity (1%).
L1063: In the evaluation of neurotoxicity, the FDA was able to confirm the results provided by the Applicant using the definition of adverse events that mapped to the Nervous System Disorder and Psychiatric SOCs and the definition of DI-CCNAE. To further evaluate neurologic toxicity, the FDA performed analyses using the definitions below, which includes the preferred terms observed in the respective safety populations for mosunetuzumab:
L1064: - Central Neurologic ToxicityMental Status Change: amnesia, cognitive disorder, confusional state, delirium, depressed level of consciousness, disturbance in attention, memory impairment, somnolence, encephalopathy, hallucination
- Aphasia, ataxia, balance disorder, dizziness, dyskinesia, gait disturbance, neurotoxicity, seizure, syncope, toxic neuropathy, tremor
- Peripheral NeuropathyBurning sensation, dysaesthesia, hyperaesthesia, hypoesthesia, neuralgia, paraesthesia, peripheral neuropathy, peripheral motor neuropathy, peripheral sensory neuropathy
L1065: ## NAE due to Nervous System Disorders in the B11 RP2D Cohort
L1066: In the B11 RP2D cohort, neurologic adverse events under the nervous system disorders SOC occurred in 39% of patients, with 3% (7/218) having Grade 3 events. There were no Grade 4
L1067: neurologic events. Grade 1 neurologic TEAEs occurred in 35% of patients (77/218), Grade 2 in 7% (16/218), and Grade 3 in 3% (7/218). The most common manifestations of NAE due to nervous system disorders were headache (21%), peripheral neuropathy (13%), and dizziness (11%). The Grade 3 events included encephalopathy, cerebrovascular accident, headache, presyncope, syncope, and toxic neuropathy. Using the grouped terms for mental status change as defined above, 6% of patients experienced mental status change, which included confusional state (4%), and disturbance in attention, cognitive disorder, encephalopathy, somnolence, and delirium at &lt;1% each. For central neurologic toxicity, excluding mental status change, tremor occurred in 2%, neurotoxicity in 1%, and ataxia, balance disorder, gait disturbance, syncope, and toxic neuropathy occurred in &lt;1% each.
L1068: There were 2 patients that experienced an event of immune effector cell associated neurotoxicity (ICANS), which is described further below.
L1069: Serious neurologic TEAEs under the nervous system disorders SOC occurred in 3% (7/218) of patients. Dose interruption due to nervous system disorders occurred in 2% (5/218) of patients. In 10% of patients in the RP2D cohort (19/218), the nervous system disorder was unresolved at the data cutoff, mostly due to the 3% incidence of unresolved headache.
L1070: ## NAE due to Nervous System Disorders in the B11 FL Cohort
L1071: Using the nervous system disorder SOC, 54% of patients in the FL cohort experienced a NAE, with one patient each (1%) having a Grade 3 event due to headache and presyncope. Grade 1 neurologic TEAEs occurred in 52% (47/90) of patients, Grade 2 in 8% (7/90), and Grade 3 in 2% (2/90). The most common manifestations of nervous system disorder were similar to that seen in the B11 RP2D cohort and included headache (33%), peripheral neuropathy (20%), and dizziness (12%). Using the grouped terms for mental status change as defined above, 4% of patients experienced mental status change characterized by confusion in 3% and a 1% incidence each of disturbance in attention and cognitive disorder.
L1072: There were no serious neurologic TEAEs due to nervous system disorders in the B11 FL cohort. There were 2 dose interruptions (2%, 2/90). In 12% of patients (11/90), the nervous system disorder was unresolved at the time of data cutoff and could be largely attributed to the incidence of peripheral neuropathy in 7% (6/90) and headache in 6% (5/90) of patients.
L1073: ## NAE due to Psychiatric System Disorders in the B11 RP2D Cohort
L1074: Neurologic adverse events under the psychiatric system disorders SOC occurred in 19% of patients in the B11 RP2D cohort. Grade 1 events occurred in 12% of patients (27/218) and grade 2 events in 7% (7/218). The single Grade 3 event of confusional state under psychiatric system disorder SOC was included in the analysis of mental status change described above. The most common manifestations in the B11 RP2D cohort were insomnia (11%) and anxiety (3%).
L1075: Only 1 patient experienced a serious TEAE due to a psychiatric system disorder, which was due to fear. There were no dose interruptions due to a psychiatric system disorder. In 5% of patients (10/218), the psychiatric disorder was unresolved at the data cutoff, largely driven by the 4% of patients with unresolved insomnia.
L1076: ## NAE due to Psychiatric System Disorders in the B11 FL Cohort
L1077: In the B11 FL cohort, NAEs under the psychiatric system disorders SOC, 23% of patients in the FL cohort experienced a psychiatric system disorder. Grade 1 events occurred in 14% of patients (13/90) and Grade 2 events in 10% (9/90). The most common manifestations were insomnia (13%), anxiety (4%), and depression (3%).
L1078: There was 1 serious NAE due to a psychiatric system disorder in the B11 FL cohort due to fear. There were no dose interruptions. In 4% of patients (4/90), the psychiatric disorder was unresolved at the data cutoff, largely due to insomnia in 3% of patients.
L1079: ## ICANS
L1080: There were two patients in the B11 RP2D cohort who experienced ICANS, a Grade 1 event and a Grade 2 event. The Grade 1 event occurred in the setting of a Grade 2 CRS event and resolved within 3 days. There was no information provided by the Applicant on the manifestations of the ICANS event. The Grade 2 event occurred in the setting of Grade 4 CRS and the primary manifestation was a confusional state. The ICANS event, along with CRS, was treated with corticosteroids (and tocilizumab) and resolved within 2 days.
L1081: ## Driving Restrictions
L1082: Prior to protocol version 10 (September 26, 2019), patients treated with mosunetuzumab were advised to refrain from driving or operating dangerous machinery for the first 3 cycles of treatment. Following September 26, 2019, no baseline driving restriction was in place for patients with follicular lymphoma and an event-based restriction was in place. Essentially, any patient that developed CRS or any adverse reaction that impairs consciousness should refrain from driving or operating dangerous machinery. The table below summarizes the patients enrolled before and after September 26, 2019.
L1083: Enrolled	B11 FL
N = 90	B11 RP2D
N = 218
Before September 26, 2019	28 (31%)	102 (47%)
After September 26, 2019	62 (69%)	116 (53%)
L1084: With the initiation of the event-driven driving restrictions, no additional safety events occurred compared to the time in which the baseline driving restrictions were in place. Additionally, 53% to 69% of the patients were enrolled during the event-driven approach. Therefore, the driving restrictions to be included in the label will be consistent with the event-driven approach for patients that experience CRS or any adverse reactions that impairs consciousness.
L1085: ## **Unresolved NAE in B11 RP2D**
L1086: At the time of the safety data cutoff (August 2021), 45 patients (21%) had unresolved NAEs. Based on updated information provided from the Applicant using a data cutoff of July 8, 2022, 16 patients (7%) died with unresolved NAEs (12 due to progression of disease, 2 due to infectious adverse events, and 2 unknown), 4 patients (2%) withdrew from the study with unresolved NAEs, 17 patients (8%) still had unresolved NAEs, and 8 patients (4%) had resolving NAEs. The most common unresolved NAEs remained insomnia, peripheral neuropathy, and headache.
L1087: ## **Conclusion:**
L1088: Sixty-nine percent of patients in the B11 FL cohort and 56% of patients in the B11 RP2D cohort experienced at least one neurologic adverse event. The majority of the neurologic adverse events were grade 1 and grade 2. However, serious events occurred in 6% and Grade 3 severe events occurred in 5%. In addition, 21% of patients had unresolved NAEs at the data cutoff. The signs and symptoms of neurologic toxicity included headache, peripheral neuropathy, dizziness, and mental status change (including confusional state, disturbance in attention, cognitive disorder, delirium, encephalopathy, and somnolence). Two cases of ICANS were also observed. The most common psychiatric manifestation were insomnia and anxiety. Given the risk of neurologic toxicity, labeling should include neurologic toxicity as a Warning and Precaution, including driving restrictions, to ensure safe use of mosunetuzumab.

L1101: ## 8.2.5. Clinical Outcome Assessment (COA) Analyses Informing Safety/Tolerability
L1102: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1103: Patient-reported outcomes are discussed in Section 8.1.2.
L1104: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1105: See the FDA's assessment in Section 8.1.2.
L1106: ## 8.2.6. Safety Analyses by Demographic Subgroups
L1107: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1108: Subgroup analyses of overall frequency of adverse events by category (i.e., all grade, Grade 3-4, fatal and serious AEs, and AEs leading to withdrawal, dose interruption or modification) were conducted for the intrinsic factors of age, gender and race in Group B of Study GO29781, to yield the largest subpopulations to allow a meaningful interpretation of the results.
L1109: The frequency of adverse events by category reported during initial treatment with mosunetuzumab IV monotherapy in Group B was generally similar between patients aged 18–65 years (N=246) and those aged &gt;65 years (N=168), male (N=269) and female (N=145) patients. The safety analysis by race subgroup was conducted on White (N=320) and Asian (N=67) patients only, as the numbers of patients in other racial groups were too small to draw any meaningful conclusions. AEs for the majority of AE categories were reported in a higher proportion of patients in the White subgroup when compared to the Asian subgroup; however, the difference in the sample sizes of these subgroups could potentially be a reason for the observed imbalances. Of note, no Grade 5 PD was reported among these 67 Asian patients at the time of CCOD.
L1110: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1111: The FDA's safety analysis based on age and sex is summarized in the tables below. Safety analysis based on race subgroups was not completed due to the small numbers of racial and ethnic minority patients enrolled in the safety population for whom conclusions about safety and effectiveness could not be drawn. The numbers of patients older than 75 years of age were also too small to conduct a meaningful analysis. The limited enrollment of older patients and racial and ethnic minorities is a significant limitation. To address this, a PMR will be issued to verify the clinical benefit of mosunetuzumab in a randomized trial in patients with relapsed or refractory follicular lymphoma and the trial should enroll sufficient numbers of racial and ethnic minority patients to reflect the racial and ethnic diversity of the U.S. patient population and allow for interpretation of the results in these patients.

L1124: ## 8.2.8. Additional Safety Explorations
L1125: ## Human Carcinogenicity or Tumor Development
L1126: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1127: In accordance with the International Council for Harmonisation (ICH) guidelines ICH S9 (2009), no carcinogenicity studies have been conducted because mosunetuzumab is intended to treat patients with advanced cancer.
L1128: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1129: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. FDA notes that no carcinogenicity studies were conducted as mosunetuzumab is a therapeutic bispecific antibody being developed for oncology indications. The FDA Nonclinical Review Team agreed that carcinogenicity studies are not needed to support the approval of this BLA.
L1130: ## Human Reproduction and Pregnancy
L1131: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1132: No pregnancies were reported during the GO29781 study.
L1133: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1134: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. FDA notes that the mosunetuzumab USPI will include a Warning and Precaution for embryo-fetal toxicity.
L1135: ## Pediatrics and Assessment of Effects on Growth
L1136: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1137: Only adult patients were eligible for inclusion in Study GO29781 and all enrolled patients were \ge 18 years.
L1138: The Agency agreed to an initial Pediatric Study Plan (iPSP-2) amendment for mosunetuzumab
L1139: (b) (4)
L1140: (b) (4)
L1141: The Applicant has requested a deferral (under section 505B(a)(3)(A)(iii) of PREA)
L1142: (b) (4)
L1143: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1144: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. FDA notes that no studies were conducted in pediatric patients and the development of mosunetuzumab is focused on adult patients with B-cell non-Hodgkin lymphoma. For information on the requested deferral of pediatric investigations, see Section 10.
L1145: ## Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound
L1146: No information is currently available. There were no reported cases of overdose of mosunetuzumab in the GO29781 study.
L1147: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1148: Not applicable
L1149: ## 8.2.9. Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L1150: ## Safety Concerns Identified Through Postmarket Experience
L1151: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1152: Not applicable.
L1153: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1154: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment.
L1155: ## Expectations on Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L1156: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1157: It is expected that safety issues can be adequately managed through labeling, additional risk minimization measures and routine postmarketing surveillance.
L1158: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1159: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. In the postmarket setting, the Applicant will be required to complete an integrated safety analysis in patients with follicular lymphoma and other lymphoid malignancies to further characterize the incidence and severity of neurologic adverse reactions, hematologic adverse reactions, hemophagocytic lymphohistiocytosis, infections, and pneumonitis/interstitial lung disease, that may develop with longer term use of mosunetuzumab.
L1160: ## Integrated Assessment of Safety
L1161: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1162: The overall safety profile of mosunetuzumab at the RP2D/intended registration dose appears manageable with no unexpected safety signals, considering the advanced nature of the disease and the heavily pretreated patient population under study. The AE profile consisted of CRS (majority were Grade 1-2, limited to Cycle 1, manageable and all resolved); hematologic and associated laboratory abnormalities (limited clinical significance, manageable and reversible); and low rates of treatment discontinuation. The current pharmacovigilance plan and product label include guidance for patient management of CRS, serious infections, tumor flare, and TLS, and these measures are considered adequate to manage the risks. The tolerability of mosunetuzumab was evident by the low proportion of patients who discontinued mosunetuzumab due to AEs. Most AEs requiring intervention could be adequately managed and resolved following dose interruptions (withholding of dose), and/or supportive care. Dose interruptions generally did not prevent patients continuing to receive their planned mosunetuzumab dose for the majority of the duration of treatment.
L1163: The mosunetuzumab safety profile demonstrates advantages over other approved treatments for R/R FL \ge2 Prior Therapies. The treatment discontinuation rate due to AEs (4.1%) is lower compared to PI3K inhibitors (15-35%) or tazemetostat (8%) (see Table 4 of Clinical Overview, Module 2.5), indicating good tolerability. The CRS profile appears to be milder than CAR-T therapy. Compared to axicabtagene ciloleucel, CRS events reported for mosunetuzumab were less frequent and less severe with fewer Grade \ge3 CRS, and the absence of fatal CRS events. Mosunetuzumab also compares favorably with axicabtagene ciloleucel with respect to neurotoxicity profile with a lower incidence and severity of neurologic adverse events, including Grade \ge3 and serious events with no fatal neurologic adverse events. Mosunetuzumab also has other advantages over axicabtagene ciloleucel, being immediately available for patients,
L1164: avoiding the ex-vivo T-cell manipulation and the resulting delay in treatment that may be prohibitive in patients with rapidly progressing disease, and offers the option to be administered in an outpatient setting. Furthermore, mosunetuzumab avoids the requirement for lymphodepleting chemotherapy and associated toxicities prior to axicabtagene ciloleucel administration.
L1165: In conclusion, the Applicant considers the benefits of mosunetuzumab summarized in Section 8.1.5 outweigh the risks and that the overall benefit-risk assessment of mosunetuzumab for patients with R/R FL who received \ge 2 prior therapies, based on the results from Study GO29781, to be positive. Mosunetuzumab IV monotherapy offers a new treatment option with a novel mechanism of action and demonstrates clinically meaningful advantages over approved therapies for the treatment of patients with R/R FL \ge 2 prior therapies, a patient population with a high unmet medical need, who have rapidly progressing disease and need for urgent disease control.
L1166: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1167: In general, the FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. The safety data support that mosunetuzumab demonstrated a tolerable safety profile in patients with relapsed or refractory B-cell NHL, including follicular lymphoma. However, based on the FDA's evaluation of the data, the identified risks of neurotoxicity, including ICANS, and cytopenias are warranted to include as Warnings and Precautions in labeling. Conversely, the FDA disagreed with (b) (4) Finally, given the median exposure of 8 cycles in the B11 RP2D cohort, there is limited safety data in patients that receive up to 17 cycles of mosunetuzumab treatment.
L1168: ## 9 SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS
L1169: ## 9.1. Statistical Issues
L1170: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1171: Complete response rate is pre-specified as the primary endpoint in the protocol and SAP; however, FDA considers that the efficacy is established on the basis of ORR and duration of response as assessed by an independent review facility according to standard criteria for NHL (Cheson 2007). For the B11 FL RP2D expansion cohort, the ORR rate is 80% (95% CI: 70.3%, 87.7%) and median DOR is 22.8 months (95% CI: 9.7, not estimable) based on a CCOD of 15 March 2021.
L1172: Additionally, FDA does not agree that the comparison of the CR rate based on Study GO29781 against the proposed historical control rate of (b) (4)% is inferential. Comparison of response rates from single-arm trials against a historical rate is challenging and should only be considered
L1173: supportive.
L1174: ## 9.2. Conclusions and Recommendations
L1175: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1176: The benefit-risk assessment is favorable for mosunetuzumab, a bispecific CD20 directed CD3 T cell engager indicated for the treatment of adult patients with relapsed or refractory follicular lymphoma after two or more lines of systemic therapy.
L1177: ## Efficacy:
L1178: Efficacy of mosunetuzumab is based on the results from GO29781, an open-label, multicenter, multicohort study in 90 patients with relapsed or refractory follicular lymphoma who had received at least two prior therapies, including an anti-CD20 monoclonal antibody and an alkylating agent.
L1179: Patients received step-up doses of 1 mg on Cycle 1 Day 1 and 2 mg on Cycle 1 Day 8, followed by 60 mg on Cycle 1 Day 15, and 60 mg on Cycle 2 Day 1, then 30 mg every 3 weeks in subsequent cycles. A treatment cycle was 21 days. Mosunetuzumab was administered for 8 cycles unless patients experienced progressive disease or unacceptable toxicity. After 8 cycles, patients with a complete response discontinued therapy; patients with a partial response or stable disease continued treatment up to 17 cycles, unless patients experienced progressive disease or unacceptable toxicity.
L1180: Efficacy was established on the basis of objective response rate and duration of response as assessed by an independent review facility according to standard criteria for NHL (Cheson 2007). Among the 90 patients, the ORR per IRF was 80% (95% CI: 70, 88), with 60% achieving a complete response. With a median follow-up for DOR of 14.9 months, the estimated median DOR was 22.8 months (95% CI: 10, not reached). Of the 72 responders, 62% remained in response at 12 months and 57% at 18 months.
L1181: ## Safety:
L1182: The safety profile of mosunetuzumab was primarily supported by analysis of 218 patients with B-cell NHL that received the recommended dose and regimen (1/2/60/30). The median duration of treatment was 8 cycles (range 1 to 17), with 52% exposed for at least 8 cycles. The most common adverse events (\ge 20\%) were cytokine release syndrome (39%), fatigue (36%), rash (34%), pyrexia (24%), and headache (21%). The most common Grade 3 to 4 laboratory abnormalities (\ge 10\%) were decreased lymphocyte count (92%), decreased phosphate (41%), increased glucose (40%), decreased neutrophil count (38%), increased uric acid (15%), decreased white blood cell count (22%), decreased hemoglobin (19%), and decreased platelets (12%). Serious adverse events occurred in 52%, most often due to CRS (21%), pneumonia (5%), pyrexia (5%), sepsis (3%), and a 2% incidence each of tumor flare, urinary tract infection, pleural effusion, and renal insufficiency. Adverse events leading to treatment interruption occurred in
L1183: 33%, most often due to neutropenia, infection, and CRS. Adverse events leading to treatment discontinuation occurred in 4%, most often due to CRS. Thus, the primary safety issues identified with mosunetuzumab include CRS, neurologic toxicity (including ICANS), infections, cytopenias, and tumor flare.
L1184: ## Benefit-Risk:
L1185: Mosunetuzumab has an overall favorable benefit-risk in patients with relapsed or refractory follicular lymphoma after two or more lines of systemic therapy. In the 90 patients with relapsed or refractory FL enrolled in the GO29781 study, the median age was 60 years (range: 29 to 90 years), 33% were 65 years of age or older, 61% were male, 82% were White, 9% were Asian, 4% were Black or African American, and 8% were Hispanic or Latino. A total of 77% of patients had Stage III-IV disease, 34% had bulky disease, and all patients had an Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group (ECOG) performance status of 0 or 1. The median number of prior therapies was 3 (range: 2 to 10), with 38% receiving 2 prior therapies, 31% receiving 3 prior therapies, and 31% receiving more than 3 prior therapies.
L1186: In this population, the overall response rate of 80%, with 60% achieving complete response, with durability and a tolerable safety profile constitutes a meaningful clinical benefit and a favorable benefit-risk evaluation. To support accelerated approval, the efficacy data are considered in the context of available therapy for the patients with relapsed or refractory follicular lymphoma in the 3rd line setting and beyond. Available therapy includes anti-CD20 therapy (i.e., rituximab and obinutuzumab) as a single agent and in combination, bendamustine monotherapy and in combination, and immunotherapy combinations such as rituximab plus lenalidomide. In the 3rd line setting and beyond, the ORRs for these therapies, including single-agent and combinations, ranges from 57% to 80% with associated durability. In the 90 patients with FL treated with mosunetuzumab, all patients received prior anti-CD20 monoclonal antibody and an alkylating agent and the median number of prior therapies was 3. A total of 79% of patients were refractory to prior anti-CD20 monoclonal antibody therapy, 53% were refractory to both anti-CD20 monoclonal antibody and alkylator therapy, 9% received prior rituximab plus lenalidomide therapy, 21% received prior autologous stem cell transplant, and 3% received prior CAR T therapy. Therefore, the overall response of 80% with associated durability for mosunetuzumab provides data to support a clinically meaningful benefit in the context of available therapy for patients with relapsed or refractory follicular lymphoma in the 3rd line setting and beyond.
L1187: Thus, the benefit-risk of mosunetuzumab is deemed favorable to support accelerated approval for the following indication:
L1188: - Treatment of adult patients with relapsed or refractory follicular lymphoma after two or more lines of systemic therapy
L1189: ## Confirmatory Trial
L1190: The Applicant has an ongoing randomized trial in patients with relapsed or refractory follicular lymphoma. Study GO42909 is an open-label, multicenter, randomized (1:1) trial evaluating mosunetuzumab in combination with lenalidomide compared to rituximab in combination with lenalidomide in approximately 400 patients with relapsed or refractory follicular lymphoma after at least 1 prior line of systemic therapy. The primary endpoint is progression-free survival per an independent review committee. The secondary endpoints include complete response rate, overall response rate, overall survival, patient-reported outcomes, and time to next treatment.
L1191: As of August 31, 2022, 184 patients have been enrolled out of the planned 400 patients. The FDA considers this ongoing trial to be supportive of accelerated approval for the indication described above. A PMR will be issued based on this trial to verify and describe the clinical benefit of mosunetuzumab in patients with relapsed or refractory follicular lymphoma (Section 14).

L1203: ## 11 Pediatrics
L1204: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1205: see Section 8.2.9.
L1206: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1207: Mosunetuzumab is a CD20-directed CD3 T-cell bi-specific antibody that causes the release of proinflammatory cytokines and induces lysis of B-cells. The molecular target of CD20 is relevant to pediatric patients with mature B-cell lymphoma. Therefore, per the Pediatric Research Equity Act (PREA) as amended by the FDA Reauthorization Act (FDARA), the agreed initial Pediatric Study Plan (iPSP) for mosunetuzumab included a plan, based on the molecular target of CD20, to request a partial waiver (b) (4) and a deferral (b) (4).
L1208: (b) (4) The mosunetuzumab BLA (b) (4) included a pediatric study plan with a request for a partial waiver (b) (4).
L1209: Therefore, the Agency will grant a full waiver for pediatric assessment requirements under PREA, as amended by FDARA, for mosunetuzumab for all pediatric age groups based on the rarity of pediatric patients with relapsed or refractory mature B-cell NHL making pediatric studies impossible or highly impracticable.

L1226: ## 14 Postmarketing Requirements and Commitment
L1227: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1228: The review team recommends two postmarketing requirements (PMR), an accelerated approval PMR to verify clinical benefit and a safety PMR for further characterization of safety with longer-term use of mosunetuzumab.
L1229: 1) Conduct a randomized clinical trial in patients with relapsed or refractory follicular lymphoma, with patients randomized to receive mosunetuzumab in combination with lenalidomide or rituximab in combination with lenalidomide. The primary endpoint should be progression-free survival, with secondary endpoints that include response rate and overall survival. The trial should enroll a sufficiently representative study population to reflect the racial and ethnic diversity of the U.S. patient population with follicular lymphoma and allow for interpretation of the results in these patient populations.
L1230: Final Protocol Submission: 09/2021 (completed)
L1231: Trial Completion: 06/2025
L1232: Final Report Submission: 12/2025
L1233: 2) Conduct an integrated safety analysis in patients with follicular lymphoma and other lymphoid malignancies to further characterize the incidence and severity of neurologic adverse reactions, hematologic adverse reactions, hemophagocytic lymphohistiocytosis, infections, and pneumonitis/interstitial lung disease, that may develop with longer term use of mosunetuzumab. Include patient-level and pooled analyses of ongoing and completed clinical trials and postmarketing reports and/or literature reports.
L1234: The timetable you submitted on November 22, 2022, states that you will conduct this trial according to the following schedule:
