L1294: ## 20.4. OCP Appendices (Technical documents supporting OCP recommendations)
L1295: ## 20.4.1. Summary of Bioanalytical Method
L1296: The FDA's Assessment:
L1297: Mosunetuzumab concentrations in human serum were determined using a validated enzyme-linked immunosorbent assay (ELISA).
L1298: In the assay, a monoclonal anti-idiotypic antibody against the anti-CD20 arm of mosunetuzumab was used for capture. A biotin-conjugated monoclonal anti-idiotypic antibody against the anti-CD3 arm of mosunetuzumab was applied for detection. Streptavidin (SA)-horseradish peroxidase (HRP) and a peroxidase substrate (tetramethyl benzidine) were added sequentially for color development. The lower limit of quantification (LLOQ) of the assay is 10 ng/mL. This assay was validated at two sites for analyzing samples from Study GO29781. Results for the methods validation, cross-validation and method performance during study sample analysis are provided in Table 52 and Table 53 below.
L1299: Table 52: Methods validation and performance in study GO29781
L1300: Bioanalytical method validation report name, amendments, and hyperlinks	Genentech: ELISA for Measuring BTCT4465A (Anti-CD20/CD3 TDB) Antibody in Human Serum (B20T3.003_AVR_0)
(b) (4) Partial Validation of an ELISA Method for the Quantification of Anti-CD20/CD3 TDB (BTCT4465A) in Human Serum and Addendum 1 and 2 (S84, S84-1 and S84-2)
Method description	The method for the quantification of mosunetuzumab in human serum is an ELISA. A standard curve is prepared fresh in standard diluent (2% normal human serum in sample diluent). Quality controls and unknown samples are diluted to the assay minimum required dilution of 1/50 in sample diluent. Plate is incubated with mouse anti-BTCT4465A monoclonal antibody (against to anti-CD20 Fab) in carbonate buffer and then washed and blocked with assay diluent. The calibrators, diluted quality controls, and samples are transferred to the coated and blocked plate and incubated at room temperature. Following an additional wash step, a solution of biotinylated mouse anti-BTCT4465A monoclonal antibody (against to anti-CD3 Fab) is added and incubated at room temperature. Unbound materials are removed with a wash step. Subsequently, streptavidin conjugated to HRP is added for detection and incubated. Unbound materials are removed with a wash step. Then, a peroxidase substrate TMB is added for color development before stopping with 1M phosphoric acid. The plate is read at 450 nm for detection absorbance and at 630 nm for reference absorbance.
The LLOQ to ULOQ neat serum concentration range is from 10.0 to 500 ng/mL. The in-well calibration range is from 0.125 to 16.0 ng/mL. A four-parameter logistic, unweighted, least-squares regression algorithm is used to quantify unknown samples.

L1351: ## 20.4.3. Mosunetuzumab Pharmacodynamics
L1352: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1353: ## B-cell Depletion
L1354: To assess the magnitude and kinetics of B-cell depletion, a quantitative flow cytometry assay to detect CD19+ B cells was added to the Study Protocol (version 9 [dated 30 January 2019]). B-cell depletion was measured in 152 patients from cohort B11. A majority (110/152 patients, 72%) of patients enrolled had B-cell counts below 70 cells/\muL (the lower limit of the normal range for peripheral B cell counts) at baseline, and approximately half of patients (71/152, 47%) enrolled had B-cell counts below the level of quantitation (LLOQ) for the assay (5 cells/\muL) at baseline.
L1355: For patients with paired baseline and on-study samples, i.e., those having measurable B-cells (\ge5 cells/\muL) prior to mosunetuzumab dosing and at least one additional measurement post-treatment, B-cells were found to be reduced to undetectable levels in most patients within Cohort B11 as early as Cycle 2 [58/63 (92%)], and depletion was sustained at later cycles including at Cycle 4 (58/60, 97%), and Cycle 8 (45/48, 94%) (Table 58). Furthermore, 100% of these patients had B-cell depletion to &lt;70 cells/\muL by cycle 4.

L1359: ## IgG Levels
L1360: Of the 105 evaluable patients in the B11 RP2D cohort (n=218) who had a baseline IgG measurement and at least one post-baseline IgG measurement, a total of 38 patients enrolled had IgG levels &lt;5 g/L, the remaining 67 patients had baseline IgG levels \ge 5 g/L.
L1361: - Among 38 patients with baseline IgG levels &lt;5 g/L, 78.9% (30/38) of the patients maintained post-baseline IgG levels &lt;5 g/L.
- Among 67 patients with baseline IgG levels \ge5 g/L, 59.7% (40/67) of the patients maintained post-baseline IgG levels \ge5 g/L, while the remaining patients (27/67; 40.3%) had the IgG values decreased to &lt;5 g/L.
L1362: ## Cytokine levels
L1363: Cytokines (IL-2, IL-6, IL-10, TNF-\gamma, IFN-\alpha) were measured in clinical studies GO29781. Only IL-6 and IFN\gamma were cytokines that were measured in all cohorts, including the Group A and B step-up dosing cohorts. For TNF-\alpha, IL-2 and IL-10, data are only available for Group A and cohort B11 in Group B.
L1364: The general profile of all cytokines showed a consistent pattern of acute elevation after initial mosunetuzumab exposure in both Group A (fixed-dosing) and Group B (step-up-dosing) followed by a return to baseline levels later within Cycle 1. In Group A, no changes from baseline were observed in later cycles (Figure 26). In the step-up-dosing Group B, a first cytokine peak was observed after the first step dose on Cycle 1 Day 1, and a second cytokine peak was observed with the first loading dose on Cycle 1 Day 15. However, two peaks were not found to be proportional to mosunetuzumab dose (Figure 27). Limited data are available in later cycles. This repeat dose and time-related decrease observed within tested cytokines that coincide with CRS occurrence reflect the outcome of effective CRS mitigation by the proposed step-up dosing regimen of 1/2/60/30 mg.

L1366: Source: figure 8 in response to FDA information request received on 08/22/2022 and figure 1 in exposure response for biomarker report 1112127.

L1369: ## 20.4.4. Population PK Analysis for Mosunetuzumab
L1370: ## 20.4.4.1. Executive Summary
L1371: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1372: In support of the proposed dosing regimen of mosunetuzumab in adult patients with relapsed or refractory (R/R) follicular lymphoma (FL) who have received at least two prior systemic therapies, the Applicant submitted the Population Pharmacokinetic (PopPK) report from the IV monotherapy cohorts of Study GO29781, including the fixed dosing group (Group A; 0.05-2.8 mg IV q3w) and the step-up dosing group (Group B; 0.4/1/2.8-1/2/60 mg IV q3w) in patients
L1373: with R/R NHL. The Applicant's final PopPK models of mosunetuzumab is characterized as 2-compartment PPK model with time varying clearance, which is adequate to describe the mosunetuzumab serum concentration-time profiles of R/R FL patients, following the IV administration of mosunetuzumab with 1/2/60/30 mg Q3W. The shrinkage value of the Applicant's mosunetuzumab PopPK model was 6.69% for IIV on CL, 6.64% for IIV on V1, 22.8% for V2, and 32.1% for distributional clearance Q. Inter-individual variabilities (IIV) were estimated to be higher for CLbase (63%) and half-life HLtrans (86%) versus CLss (18%), V1 (31%), and V2 (25%); Shrinkage was relatively low on CLbase, V1, and CLss, supporting a robust covariate analysis on these parameters, and indicating there was no obvious bias in the parameter estimates for mosunetuzumab.
L1374: Covariate analysis results showed the statistically significant covariates e.g., body weight, tumor burden, albumin, baseline anti-CD20 drug concentrations (specifically residual rituximab [R] or Gazyva [G]), and sex had a marginal impact on mosunetuzumab pharmacokinetics when assessing the influence of extreme values. In addition, neither patient age, renal impairment status, hepatic impairment status, drug material used, histology (i.e., FL vs. DLBCL vs. other), nor race (Asian vs. non-Asian) were identified as covariates influencing mosunetuzumab PK.
L1375: Given the unique step-up dose regimen, and time dependent clearance, additional simulations including PopPK model-predicted mosunetuzumab concentration-time profiles, receptor occupancy (RO)-time (%), and CL-time (L/day) profiles based on the 1/2/60/30 mg dose regimen were performed to support the AUC endpoints selected, and E-R analysis for CRS. The final PK model is acceptable for simulating post-hoc exposure metrics of mosunetuzumab (AUC0-42, AUC0-63, AUC0-84) and RO(%) used for subsequent E-R analyses for efficacy and safety measurements.
L1376: In general, the Applicant's PopPK analysis for mosunetuzumab is considered acceptable for the purpose of supporting analyses objectives. The Applicant's analyses were verified by the reviewer, with no significant discordance identified. More specifically, the developed model is acceptable to be used to derive exposure metrics for E-R analysis, and to support the Applicant's proposed labeling statements about general PK information including intrinsic and extrinsic factors in the current submission.
L1377: ## 20.4.4.2. PK Assessment Summary

L1379: Population PK (PopPK) analysis for mosunetuzumab was conducted using 7250 PK samples from 439 subjects across mosunetuzumab intravenous (IV) doses ranging with varying administration schedules: Group A (dose ranging from 0.05-2.8 mg) and Group B (dose ranging from 0.4/1.0/2.8 mg to 1.0/2.0/60 mg, including 1.0/2.0/60/30 mg). A brief overview of the data used for Mosunetuzumab PopPK analysis was listed in Figure 28. Summary statistics of the
L1380: continuous and categorical covariates that were evaluated in the PopPK analysis are shown in Table 59 and Table 60, respectively.

L1396: The Applicant claimed that a 2-compartment PopPK model with a time-varying clearance (CL) parameterized as an initial baseline clearance (CL_{base}) that transitions to a CL_{ss} according to a transition time half-life (HL_{trans}) as described in the following equation:
L1397: CL = CL_{base} + (CL_{ss} - CL_{base}) \cdot \left\{ 1 - \exp \left[ - \left( \frac{\ln(2)}{HL_{trans}} \right) \cdot t \right] \right\}
L1398: The final PK model parameter estimates and the corresponding 95% confidence intervals (CIs) from Bootstrap for mosunetuzumab are presented in Table 61. The goodness-of-fit plots for the final PK model are presented in Figure 29. The prediction corrected visual predictive check
L1399: (pcVPC) based on all data (Figure 30) illustrated the prediction percentiles and corresponding 95% CI of simulated concentrations overlaid on the observed mosunetuzumab concentrations and the corresponding 5th and 95th percentiles.

L1420: Among the covariates evaluated, body weight (with median 77.9 kg, range from 37.1 to 163 kg), tumor burden (with median 2970 \text{mm}^2, range from 96.0 to 70900 \text{mm}^2), albumin (with median 39.0 g/L, range from 19.0 to 480 g/L), baseline residual anti-CD20 drug concentrations, and sex (Female 155 [35.3%] and Male 284 [64.7%]) were found to be statistically significant covariates on mosunetuzumab PK with marginal impact on drug exposures. There were no clinically significant differences in the PK of mosunetuzumab based on age (19 to 96 years), sex, race (Asian [17.5%] and Non-Asian [82.5%]), ethnicity (Hispanic/Latino [6.2%] and not Hispanic/Latino [91.3%]), mild or moderate renal impairment (estimated Creatinine clearance [CrCL] by Cockcroft-Gault formula: 30 to 89 mL/min), or mild hepatic impairment (total bilirubin less than or equal to upper limit of normal [ULN] with AST greater than ULN or total bilirubin greater than 1 to 1.5 times ULN with any AST). The relative importance of covariate effects included in the final mosunetuzumab model was evaluated with a forest plot of relative changes in exposure (\text{AUC}_{0-42}) in the Figure 31.

L1442: ## **Special Population**
L1443: ## **Renal impairment**
L1444: The covariate analysis results showed that CrCL was not statistically significant covariates on mosunetuzumab CL. The impact of the degree of renal impairment on mosunetuzumab CL was further assessed by categorizing patients with varying degrees of renal impairment into 4 categories based on their estimated CrCL (Normal: \text{CrCL} \ge 90 \text{ mL/min}, Mild: 60 to &lt; 90 \text{ mL/min}, Moderate: 30 to &lt; 60 \text{ mL/min}, and Severe: \text{CrCL} &lt; 30 \text{ mL/min}). There is marginally increased AUC in patients with moderate and severe renal impairment compared to mild renal impairment and normal renal function (Figure 36). The Applicant claimed that the PK exposure is not expected to be impacted by renal impairment.

L1450: ## **Hepatic impairment**
L1451: The PK of mosunetuzumab is not expected to be impacted by hepatic impairment (Figure 37). Mosunetuzumab has not been investigated in subjects with severe hepatic impairment and the data from subjects with moderate hepatic impairment (Figure 37) are limited (n=2).

L1457: ## Receptor Occupancy (RO) for Mosunetuzumab
L1458: Due to varying levels of residual rituximab and/or obinutuzumab, especially in patients with aNHL, the mosunetuzumab receptor occupancy (%RO) has been used to address the confounding effect of the ER relationship for efficacy and cytokine release syndrome (CRS). The observed mosunetuzumab CD20 %RO time-course was calculated within the NONMEM using Equation below:
L1459: \%RO(t) = 100 \cdot \frac{100 \cdot Mosun(t)}{Mosun(t) + K_{D,Mosun} + \left(\frac{K_{D,Mosun}}{K_{D,Ritux}}\right) \cdot Ritux(t) + \left(\frac{K_{D,Mosun}}{K_{D,Obinut}}\right) \cdot Obinut(t)}
L1460: Here, Mosun(t), Ritux(t), and Obinut(t) are the model-predicted time-courses (ng/mL) for mosunetuzumab, rituximab, and obinutuzumab, respectively. K_{D,Mosun} (10200 ng/mL), K_{D,Ritux} (675 ng/mL), and K_{D,Obinut} (600 ng/mL) are the equilibrium dissociation constants for mosunetuzumab, rituximab and obinutuzumab, respectively, per Scatchard assay data on file.
L1461: The observed average mosunetuzumab CD20 receptor occupancy over 42 days (RO_{\text{avg}0-42}) was calculated within the NONMEM code by implementing a reservoir compartment in which receptor occupancy accumulated over time, denoted by the cumulative area under the receptor occupancy time curve (AUC_{RO}). The average %RO was then calculated by AUC_{RO}/\text{time} for any selected time point.
L1462: The PopPK model-predicted mosunetuzumab concentration-time (ug/mL), receptor occupancy (RO)-time (%), and CL-time (L/day) profiles based on the cohort B11 (N=214) from the popPK model estimates for the 1/2/60/30 mg dose regimen together with model-predicted rituximab concentration-time (ug/mL) profiles are shown in Figure 38. Given the unique step-up dosing regimen, and time dependent clearance, these figures were provided to illustrate the complexities of this PK system, and to support the AUC endpoints selected. As shown, CL decreases with time, from a typical CL_{\text{base}} of ~1.08 L/day to a CL_{\text{ss}} of ~0.584 L/day. Given the typical HL_{\text{trans}} of 16.3 days, 90% of steady state will be reached after approximately 3.3 half-lives, i.e., 54 days; this is confirmed by the blue line representing the typical mosunetuzumab concentration for the dosing regimen of 1/2/60/30 mg.
L1463: **Figure 38: Model-Predicted Mosunetuzumab Concentration- (blue), RO-(grey), CL- (red), and Rituximab Concentration-Time (green) Profiles for 1/2/60/30 mg Dosing Regimen Based on Cohort B11 Patient EBEs (N=214)**
L1464: CL= clearance at steady state.
L1465: Shaded regions for each curve represent the 25th and 75th percentiles. Solid or stippled lines represent the median value. For the rituximab time-course (green), the lower bound is set by the BLQ concentration of 0.5 \mu\text{g/mL}, which is the median for the FL patient population.
L1466: Source: Applicant's Population PK Report (poppk-1112117), Figure 32.
L1467: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1468: In general, the Applicant's PopPK analysis is considered acceptable.
L1469: ## 20.4.5. Exposure-Response Analysis
L1470: ## 20.4.5.1. ER (efficacy) Executive Summary
L1471: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1472: In support of the current BLA application, the Applicant submitted exposure-response (E-R) for efficacy analysis including complete response rate (CRR), objective response rate (ORR) and PFS with R/R NHL patients from Studies GO29781 to characterize E-R for efficacy relationships. Under the proposed dosage of 1/2/60/30 mg, the E-R efficacy analysis indicated that clinical responses (CR, ORR) increased with increasing mosunetuzumab exposure (AUC_{0-42}) and approached a plateau. Further ER analysis showed a longer median PFS with increased
L1473: mosunetuzumab exposure (AUC_{0-42}), and a longer median PFS at the 1/2/60/30 mg dose (B11 cohort, RP2D) compared to the 1/2/13.5 mg dose (B7 cohort). Loading doses of 60 mg on Cycle 1 Day 15 and Cycle 2 Day 1 were predicted by E-R analysis to achieve a plateau for clinical endpoints ORR and CR.
L1474: Overall, the Applicant's E-R analysis for efficacy appear adequate. The E-R relationship for efficacy endpoints may imply that the effectiveness of mosunetuzumab reached a plateau at the proposed dosing regimen of 1/2/60/30 mg, which supports the proposed dosage.
L1475: ## 20.4.5.2. ER (efficacy) Assessment Summary
L1476: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1477: The Applicant's E-R for efficacy analysis of Mosunetuzumab were conducted in 159 patients with relapsed or refractory B-cell Follicular Lymphoma (R/R FL) patients with \ge 2 Prior Therapies in Study GO29781 in one of the following two dosing regimens: 1) every 3 weeks (q3w) fixed dosing schedule (Group A) or 2) on a Cycle 1 step-up dosing regimen (Group B), followed by a once every 3 weeks (q3w) fixed dosing schedule. The dose range for these patients was from 0.2 mg to 1/2/60/30 mg; specifically, 7 patients from Group A cohorts received doses ranging from 0.2 to 2.8 mg q3w, and 152 patients from Group B step-up dosing cohorts received doses ranging from 0.4/1.0/2.8 mg to 1.0/2.0/60/30 mg.
L1478: A brief overview of E-R analysis for Mosunetuzumab is listed in the Table 62: and Figure 39. The efficacy endpoints for E-R analyses primarily included CR, OR, PFS, DOCR, and DOR in R/R FL patients with \ge 2 Prior Therapies. The E-R analysis for R/R FL covered age ranging from 27 to 90 years with a median of 60 years, body weight ranging from 42 to 140 kg with a median of 80.4 kg, and albumin ranging from 20 to 480 g/L with a median of 41 g/L. Table 63: shows statistical summaries of continuous and categorical covariates for the efficacy E-R population.

Horizontal lines at the bottom represent simulated AUC distributions (median with 95% CI) at selected dose levels (green = 1/2/60/30 mg; yellow=1/2/30 mg; black=1/2/13.5 mg); simulations are based on the popPK model EBEs.
L1504: Source: Applicant's Exposure Response Analysis Report (ER-1112149), Figure 8.
L1505: At the proposed dose of 1/2/60/30 mg, the ER model-predicted probabilities for CRR and ORR were 63.1% [49.7–75.0%; 95% PI] and 79.1% [69.1–87.7%; 95% PI], respectively. When compared to the maximal CR and OR rates, the model predictions for the proposed 1/2/60/30 mg regimen are at the plateau of the logistic regression Emax ER curves.
L1506: The covariate analysis indicated that baseline tumor size and predose anti-CD20 drug concentrations were predictors for E50 and Emax, respectively; patients with larger tumors or with high predose aCD20 concentrations are predicted to have a lower probability to attain CR, though still expected to be higher than 50% CR rate (Figure 42).

L1523: ## 20.4.5.3. ER (safety) Executive Summary
L1524: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1525: In support of the dosage recommendations for this BLA, the Applicant also submitted reports of exposure-response (E-R) for safety analysis from patients with R/R NHL from Studies GO29781 to characterize E-R for safety relationships. The E-R for safety results indicated that the no positive E-R relationship for any AEs Grade \ge 3 and any serious AE with PK exposure to the time of event in the pooled population across all evaluated doses. The Applicant's E-R for safety analysis results using ROmax % supports the step-up dosing regimen at 1/2/60/30 mg with observed low frequency of Grade &gt; 2 CRS. Following the step-up dosing regimen did not lead to exposure-related increases in rates and onset time of Grade &gt; 3 neutropenia, Grade \ge 3 infections or infestations, any Grade \ge 3 AEs, or any Grade &gt; 1 AEs.
L1526: In addition, the Applicant also conducted E-R analysis to support neurological AE (NAE) risk mitigation through step-up dosing regimens where there was a lack of ER relationships for frequency or onset of NAE despite dose escalation of the target doses administered on Cycle 1 Day 15, Cycle 2 Day 1, and every 3 weeks thereafter. No exposure-dependency for DI-CCNAE (driving-impacting cognition or consciousness neurologic adverse event) in R/R NHL was observed across the dose range tested (i.e., 0.4/1/2.8 mg to 1/2/60/30 mg) using the step-up dosing regimen. The Applicant's E-R for NAE analysis appear reasonable.
L1527: Overall, the Applicant's E-R for safety analysis appear adequate to support the proposed dosing regimen (1/2/60/30 mg).
L1528: ## 20.4.5.4. ER (safety) Assessment Summary
L1529: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1530: The Applicant's E-R for safety analysis of Mosunetuzumab (Mosunetuzumab) was conducted with 439 patients with relapsed or refractory B-cell non-Hodgkin's lymphoma (NHL) patients in Study GO29781. A brief overview of E-R analysis for Mosunetuzumab is listed in the Table 62: and Figure 39. The dose range for these patients was from 0.05 mg to 1/2/60 mg, including 1/2/60/30 mg. Adverse Events Grade \ge 2 CRS, Grade \ge 3 neutropenia, Grade \ge 3 infections and infestations, any Grade \ge 3 event, and any Grade \ge 1 event in R/R NHL patients were included in E-R for safety analysis. The data included 32 patients from Group A cohorts receiving doses ranging from 0.05 to 2.8 mg every three weeks (q3w), and 407 patients from Group B step-up dosing cohorts receiving doses ranging from 0.4/1.0/2.8 mg to 1.0/2.0/60 mg, including 1.0/2.0/60/30 mg.
L1531: The E-R safety analysis for R/R NHL covered age ranging from 19 to 96 years with a median of 63 years, body weight ranging from 37.1 to 163 kg with a median 77.9 kg, and albumin ranging from 20 to 480 g/L with a median of 41 g/L. Table 64: shows statistical summaries of baseline continuous and categorical covariates for the evaluable patients in the E-R for safety (E-S) analysis.
L1532: For safety E-R of CRS, a mosunetuzumab receptor occupancy (RO)-based exposure endpoint - a ROmax% metric (instead of AUC or Cmax) was used to address the confounding issue of the ER relationship for CRS incidence from varying levels of anti-CD20 drug concentrations (specifically residual rituximab [R] or Gazyva [G]).

L1561: ## Exposure Response for Neurological Adverse Events (NAEs)
L1562: The applicant also conducted the mosunetuzumab exposure-response (ER) analyses (including logistic regression and TTE analyses) for NAEs (defined as adverse events reported as any primary or secondary Preferred Terms [PT] in either the System Organ Class [SOC] "Nervous system disorders" or SOC "Psychiatric disorders") based on a more recent clinical dataset (clinical cutoff date [CCOD] of 27 Aug 2021) and DI-CCNAE (driving-impacting cognition or consciousness neurologic adverse event), including 439 pharmacokinetic-evaluable patients (438 non-Hodgkin's lymphoma [NHL] patients).
L1563: The exposure metrics evaluated included area under the mosunetuzumab concentration-time curve (AUC) and CD20 receptor occupancy (RO) based metrics over pre-defined time windows of 0-7 days, 0-14 days, and 0-42 days. A summary of common NAEs (\ge 2\%) by preferred term is summarized in Table 66. Overall results of the ER analyses for NAE patients in the safety evaluable population are shown in Figure 47. For Kaplan-Meier analyses, exposures were stratified by tertiles of observed values, the TTE analyses of any-grade NAE onset are shown in Figure 48. The logistic regression ER analyses indicate a slightly positive ER slope for increased
L1564: NAEs that occurs with AUC_{0-42} and RO_{max_{0-42}} (p&gt;0.05). And Kaplan-Meier analyses showed similar trend with a visual separation occurring near the timing of the higher 60 mg loading dose (day 15); however, overall log-rank tests for time-to-event analyses showed non-significant p-values for both metrics. A flat E-R relationship for NAE was observed following the Cycle 1 Day 1 (C1D1) doses of 0.05 to 2.8 mg when mosunetuzumab was administered as fixed q3w doses (i.e., Group A).
L1565: The E-R relationships with DI-CCNAE were mostly flat across the exposure range tested (Figure 49). In summary, the overall E-R data support that neurological events are mostly lower grade and have, at worst, a modest exposure-dependency for NAE and no exposure-dependency for DI-CCNAE in R/R NHL across the dose range tested (i.e., 0.4/1/2.8 mg to 1/2/60/30 mg) using the step-up dosing regimen.

L1599: ## 20.4.5.5. Exposure Response Analyses for Pharmacodynamic Biomarkers
L1600: ## Applicant's Position
L1601: To support dosing recommendation for this BLA, the Applicant also developed the exposure-response (ER) relationship for pharmacodynamic biomarkers such as interferon-gamma (IFN-\gamma) and IL-6 in study GO29781. The IFN-\gamma and IL-6 were selected as the primary pharmacodynamic (PD) biomarkers of interest for the purpose of E-R characterization as a proximal marker for target engagement as it is produced by T-cells upon activation. As shown in Figure 50, a rapid but transient increase in IFN-\gamma was observed early after the initial drug administration (i.e., within 30 minutes after the end of infusion) and elevated IFN-\gamma levels were detected up to 6 hours post end of infusion but then returned to baseline levels by 24 hours. Similar trend was also observed for IL-6 (Figure 53).
L1602: As shown in Figure 51 and Figure 52, there is no apparent dose-response relationship between R0max and PD biomarker- interferon-gamma (IFN-\gamma). No exposure-dependent increases were observed following later cycles of dose administration. Similar to ER analysis for IFN-\gamma, the ER relationship highlighted in Figure 54 and Figure 55 for IL-6 demonstrates that maximum change in the Log2-transformed IL-6 concentration (Log2-IL-6,CFBLmax) tended to increase modestly with increasing R0max within the mosunetuzumab dose range evaluated following a Cycle 1, Day 1 dose. However, the E-R relationship following the Cycle 1, Day 15 loading dose ("3rd Dose") remained relatively flat, even with higher exposures achieved with the 60 mg loading dose. Beyond the third dose in later cycles, no apparent exposure-dependent IL-6 increases were observed following mosunetuzumab administration.
L1603: This time- and repeat dose-dependent shift (i.e., reduction) in IFN-\gamma/ IL-6 ER relationship was consistent with the clinical mitigation of acute cytokine release from T cells upon repeated ascending dosing of mosunetuzumab. IL-6 is an established and generally recognized upstream marker of CRS. Hence, interpretations of IL-6 E-R data in this response continue to support the effectiveness of the proposed registration 1/2/60/30 mg mosunetuzumab Cycle 1 step-up dosing for mitigating acute cytokine-driven toxicities, where mosunetuzumab IV monotherapy is administered with escalating Cycle 1 Day 1 (C1D1), C1D8, and C1D15 dose levels, followed by 30 mg q3w dosing on Day 1 of subsequent cycles.

L1623: ## 20.4.6. Population PK Analysis for Tocilizumab and sIL-6R
L1624: ## 20.4.6.1. Executive Summary
L1625: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1626: In support of the use of tocilizumab (TCZ) for the management of CRS induced by anti-CD20 T cell engaging (TCE) bispecifics antibodies (i.e., mosunetuzumab and glofitamab), the Applicant submitted updated PopPK report with study GO29781 (Mosunetuzumab) and NP30179 (Glofitamab). The PopPK analyses were updated from the previous PK model of tocilizumab by using data from multiple studies in adult patients with RA and pediatric patients with sJIA and pcJIA.
L1627: The PK of tocilizumab was described by a two-compartment model with parallel linear and Michaelis-Menten elimination, and the first-order subcutaneous absorption. The PK of total sIL-6R was characterized by equations of quasisteady-state (QSS) approximation of the target-mediated drug disposition (TMDD) model. The final PopPK models for tocilizumab and sIL-6R were validated and the updated PopPK model is generally in line with the previous model.
L1628: The covariate analysis showed CRS had significant effects on tocilizumab PK parameters and sIL6R parameters. Different TCZ dosing regimen scenarios were simulated and the following TCZ dosing recommendations were shown to maintain TCZ concentrations below the “safety threshold” during the full treatment duration of mosunetuzumab or glofitamab treatment: Two consecutive TCZ doses at least 8 hours apart, maximum two doses per CRS event; maximum three TCZ doses within each time period of 6 weeks of mosunetuzumab or glofitamab.
L1629: The Applicant's PopPK analyses for tocilizumab and sIL-6R were verified by the reviewer, with no significant discordance identified.
L1630: (b) (4) Refer to section 8.2.4.1
L1631: for details.
L1632: ## 20.4.6.2. PPK Assessment Summary for Tocilizumab and sIL-6R

L1634: A total of 289 TCZ PK observations and 254 sIL-6R observations from 67 patients were available for the PK-sIL6R analysis. Of those, a total of 266 TCZ PK observations and 218 sIL-6R observations from 61 patients were used for model development (model development population) and the remaining ones were used for external evaluation of the model. The summary of the available data is presented in Table 67. Summary statistics of the continuous and categorical covariates that were evaluated in the PopPK analysis are shown in Table 68 and Table 69, respectively.

L1650: Source file: CatCov.csv (CRS_ReportPlots.R)
L1651: Source: Applicant's Population PK Report (poppk-1114219), Table 5.
L1652: The serum concentration-time course of tocilizumab following single and multiple IV administrations in adult patients with CRS was accurately described by a two compartment pharmacokinetic model with parallel linear and Michaelis-Menten elimination. The final PK model parameter estimates and the corresponding 95% confidence intervals (CIs) for TCZ and sIL-6R are presented in Table 70. The goodness-of-fit plots for the final PK model are presented in Figure 56. The prediction corrected visual predictive check (pcVPC) based on all data (Figure 57) illustrated the prediction percentiles and corresponding 95% CI of simulated concentrations overlaid on the observed TCZ and sIL-6R concentrations and the corresponding 5th and 95th percentiles. CRS had significant effects on tocilizumab PK parameters and sIL6R parameters. Specifically, linear clearance in patients with CRS grade 2 or higher was on average 60% (95% CI: 25-103%) higher than in patients with RA. Elimination rate of bound sIL-6R, was 109% (95% CI: 80-142%) higher in patients with CRS than in patients with RA. Degradation rate of free (unbound) sIL-6R was 76% (95% CI: 64-89%) higher in patients with CRS than in patients with RA. The CRS effect on VC was relatively minor (15% increase, 95% CI:
L1653: 3-28%). Inter-compartment clearance Q was 3.59 times higher in patients with CRS than in patients with RA (95% CI: from 3.11 to 4.14 times higher).

L1682: ## 20.4.7. Physiologically Based Pharmacokinetic Analyses Review
L1683: ## Executive Summary
L1684: The objective of this review is to evaluate the adequacy of the Applicant's physiologically based pharmacokinetic (PBPK) analyses to assess the drug-drug interaction (DDI) potential of mosunetuzumab with CYP3A sensitive substrates midazolam and simvastatin and duration of the potential effects.
L1685: (b) (4)
L1686: The Division of Pharmacometrics has reviewed the PBPK analyses report (report 1110241), the response to FDA's information requests submitted on August 22nd (seq 0039, 20220822-response), and the modeling supporting files to conclude that
L1687: (b) (4)
L1688: - The Applicant's PBPK analyses are inadequate
L1689: (b) (4)
L1690: - Based on the reviewer's evaluation of available clinical data in the literature, the magnitude of effect of mosunetuzumab administration on CYP3A activity is expected to be [REDACTED] (b) (4) to that observed in rheumatoid arthritis (RA) patients, 1.5 to 2.4-fold increase in the exposure of sensitive CYP3A substrates. CYP3A activity is estimated to take approximately [REDACTED] (b) (4) after Cycle 2 mosunetuzumab administration to return to the [REDACTED] (b) (4)% of its baseline.
L1691: ## Background
L1692: Mosunetuzumab (BTCT4465A, RO7030816) is a full-length, humanized immunoglobulin G 1 (IgG1), CD3/CD20 bispecific antibody in development for the treatment of adult patients with relapsed or refractory follicular lymphoma (R/R FL) who have received at least two prior systemic therapies. Mosunetuzumab is proposed to be administered for 8 cycles for patients who achieve a complete response, and for 17 cycles for patients who achieve partial response or have stable disease in response to the treatment. Mosunetuzumab is given every 3 weeks for the treatment duration, in which the antibody is administered via IV infusion at 1-, 2- and 60-mg on Day 1, 8 and 15 of Cycle 1 (C1D1, C1D8, C1D15), respectively, at 60-mg on Day 1 of Cycle 2 (C2D1), and at 30-mg on Day 1 of Cycle 3 and subsequent cycles.
L1693: Mosunetuzumab acts by recruitment of effector T-cells via CD3 to engage with target CD20-expressing B cells, leading to T-cell activation and T-cell mediated B cell cytolysis in a target- and dose-dependent manner (20220822-response). Cytokines including interleukin 6 (IL-6) are known to modulate CYP activities in humans. Mosunetuzumab treatment induced transient elevation of cytokines such as IL-6. This review focused on whether the Applicant's PBPK modeling is adequate to predict the magnitude and duration of drug interaction effects of mosunetuzumab on CYP3A activity via IL-6 elevation.
L1694: ## Methods
L1695: All simulations were performed using the PK/PD Profiles mode in the Simcyp® Simulator (Version 17, Certara, Sheffield, UK). Schemes of the PBPK simulation strategy are shown in Figure 60, which summarizes the model development and verification of the IL-6 PBPK model, and model applications in assessing the effect of IL-6 elevation on midazolam pharmacokinetics.
L1696: (b) (4)
L1697: ## **Is PBPK Prediction Adequate to Address the Concern of Potential Effect of Mosunetuzumab on CYP substrates?**
L1698: No, PBPK analyses are considered inadequate to evaluate drug interaction potential of mosunetuzumab and duration of its effect on CYP3A substrates because exposure-response relationships between cytokines and CYP3A activity in human have not been established. The reviewer's assessment of the PBPK analyses is detailed below.
L1699: ## **1. Uncertainty about *in-vitro-in-vivo* extrapolation of IL-6 suppression data**
L1700: Currently, an exposure-response relationship between plasma IL-6 levels and change in CYP3A activity in humans has not been established. (b) (4)
L1701: (b) (4)
L1702: (b) (4)
L1703: ## 2. *The ability of PBPK modeling to predict the time course of the effect of IL-6 on CYP3A activity has not been established*
L1704: In response to FDA's information request (seq 0039, 20220822-response), the Applicant simulated the duration of drug interaction with the CYP3A substrate midazolam caused by IL-6 elevation following mosunetuzumab treatment (1/2/60 mg at C1D1, C1D8, C1D15, and 60 mg at C2D1) (Figure 62), and concluded
L1705: (b) (4)
L1706: (b) (4)
L1707: (b) (4)
L1708: The FDA did not agree with this proposal. To date, the time course of the effects of cytokine release caused by antibody treatment on CYP enzyme activity in non-RA patients has not been studied. Therefore, the ability of PBPK modeling to predict the time course of the effect of IL-6 on CYP3A activity cannot be assessed.
L1709: (b) (4)
L1710: (b) (4)
L1711: (b) (4)
L1712: The Applicant's PBPK modeling may not capture the time course of recovery from the suppression effect triggered by mosunetuzumab administration.
L1713: (b) (4)
L1714: Source: Reviewer's analysis
L1715: ## **3. Potential underprediction of CYP3A suppression**
L1716: The in vitro CYP3A suppression parameters (E_{min}/EC50, Table 73), used to simulate interactions between tocilizumab and simvastatin and between sarilumab and midazolam in RA patients, were applied to simulate the interaction with midazolam following mosunetuzumab administration in Cycle 1.
L1717: (b) (4)
L1718: (b) (4)
L1719: ## **4. Potential effects of other factors on CYP3A cannot be ruled out**
L1720: In the PBPK report, the Applicant focused on predicting the effect of IL-6 on midazolam PK. The Applicant's rationale is that CYP3A was reported to be the enzyme most sensitive to cytokine-mediated suppression, and IL-6 was the most potent suppressor among the cytokines tested in vitro. In addition, the evidence for the impact of other cytokines on CYP activity is inconclusive and often confounded by the coexistence of significant level of IL-6 (Chen et al 2022).
L1721: (b) (4)
L1722: ## Conclusions
L1723: The proposed [REDACTED] (b) (4) cannot be supported by the PBPK analyses, which are considered inadequate due to lack of the established exposure-response relationships between cytokines and CYP3A activity in human. The effect of mosunetuzumab administration on CYP3A activity is expected to be [REDACTED] (b) (4) to that observed in RA patients, and CYP3A activity is estimated to take approximately [REDACTED] (b) (4) after Cycle 2 mosunetuzumab administration to return to the [REDACTED] (b) (4)% of its baseline.
L1724: 2/9

12. Aitken A and Morgan ET. Gene-specific effects of inflammatory cytokines on cytochrome P450 2C, 2B6 and 3A4 mRNA levels in human hepatocytes. Drug Metab Dispos. 2007 Sep;35(9):1687-93.

L1743: Figure 1 displays four graphs showing in vitro cytokine production (IL-2, IL-6, IFN-\gamma, and TNF-\alpha) as a function of antibody concentration (ng/mL) on a logarithmic scale, comparing Mosunetuzumab (blue line) and (b) (4) (red line). The Y-axis represents cytokine concentration in pg/mL. The graphs show that both agents induce similar levels of cytokine production across the tested concentration range (0.1 to 10000 ng/mL).
Note: Representative figures of 3 donors.
L1744: Figure 2 displays four graphs showing the binding of BTCT4465A (v0.1 and v0.2) to CD20 and CD3\epsilon on human and cynomolgus monkey B and T cells, respectively. The Y-axis represents Normalized MFI (Mean Fluorescence Intensity) and the X-axis represents Log [\mug/mL] of BTCT4465A concentration.
Top Left: Binding of BTCT4465A to CD20 on Human B Cells
Normalized MFI (0 to 150). Both v0.1 (red diamonds) and v0.2 (blue squares) show strong binding, reaching MFI values around 120-130 at 10^{-1} \mug/mL.
Top Right: Binding of BTCT4465A to CD3\epsilon on Human T Cells
Normalized MFI (0 to 600). Both v0.1 and v0.2 show strong binding, reaching MFI values around 500-550 at 10^{-1} \mug/mL.
Bottom Left: Binding of BTCT4465A to CD20 on Cynomolgus Monkey B Cells
Normalized MFI (0 to 400). Both v0.1 and v0.2 show strong binding, reaching MFI values around 300-350 at 10^{-1} \mug/mL.
Bottom Right: Binding of BTCT4465A to CD3\epsilon on Cynomolgus Monkey T Cells
Normalized MFI (0 to 500). Both v0.1 and v0.2 show strong binding, reaching MFI values around 400-450 at 10^{-1} \mug/mL.
The binding curves for both versions (v0.1 and v0.2) are nearly identical across all four cell types, indicating robust binding to CD20 and CD3\epsilon receptors.
The binding interactions of BTCT4465A with human Fc gamma receptors (Fc\gammaRIIIA (b) (4), Fc\gammaRIIIB, and Fc\gammaRIIIA (b) (4)) were assessed in enzyme-linked immunosorbent assay (ELISA)-based ligand binding assays (Study 14-3202). Results showed that while trastuzumab positive control binds robustly to all Fc gamma receptors tested; mosunetuzumab shows minimal binding to each Fc gamma receptor tested.
L1745: Figure 3 displays six dose-response curves showing the binding responses of BTCT4465A (blue line) and Trastuzumab (red line) to various Fc\gamma Receptors (Fc\gammaRIIA, Fc\gammaRIIB, Fc\gammaRIIIA, and Fc\gammaRIIIA (b) (4)). The Y-axis represents Average Absorbance 450-650 nm, and the X-axis represents Concentration (\mug/mL) on a logarithmic scale (0.001 to 1000). Trastuzumab consistently shows high binding affinity across all receptors, reaching maximum absorbance (around 3.5-4.0) at low concentrations (0.1 to 1 \mug/mL). BTCT4465A shows minimal binding response across all receptors, with absorbance remaining near zero, indicating significantly lower affinity compared to Trastuzumab.
L1746: A: Fc Region is Not Required for Cell Killing Mediated by (b) (4)
Graph showing % of BJAB Killing versus Antibody (pM). Two curves are plotted: Full IgG (Red) and F(ab')2 (Blue). Both curves show similar dose-dependent killing, reaching approximately 90% killing at 100 pM.
B: Cell Killing Mediated by (b) (4) is Dependent on presence of T-cells
Graph showing % of BJAB Killing versus Antibody (ng/mL). Two conditions are compared: PBMC (Red dots) and CD3+ depleted (Blue squares). PBMC shows dose-dependent killing, reaching approximately 60% at 100 ng/mL. CD3+ depleted shows minimal killing, remaining near 0%.
C: Both CD8+ and CD4+ T-Cells Can be Activated by (b) (4) Only in the Presence of CD20+ B-Cell Lines
Graph showing % of CD69+CD25+ in T cells versus antibody (ng/mL). Four conditions are compared: CD8+T + Bjab (Red), CD4+T + Bjab (Blue), CD8+T only (Black), and CD4+T only (Green). Activation is observed only when T-cells are co-incubated with Bjab cells (CD8+T + Bjab and CD4+T + Bjab), reaching approximately 85% activation at 10 ng/mL. CD8+T only and CD4+T only show negligible activation.
L1747: Figure 5 displays two dose-response graphs showing the effect of B-JAB antibody concentration on T-cell killing mechanisms.
The left graph, titled "Granzyme B (FACS)", plots % GrB+ CD8+ in T Cells (Y-axis, 0 to 30) versus Antibody (ng/mL) (X-axis, log scale, 0.01 to 1000). The CD8+ T + B-JAB condition (red line) shows high Granzyme B expression, reaching approximately 28% at 100 ng/mL. The CD4+ T + B-JAB condition (blue line) shows minimal Granzyme B expression, remaining below 5% across the tested concentration range.
The right graph, titled "Perforin (ELISA)", plots Perforin in Media (pg/mL) (Y-axis, 0 to 3500) versus Antibody (ng/mL) (X-axis, log scale, 0.01 to 1000). The CD8+ T + B-JAB condition (red line) shows high Perforin release, reaching approximately 3200 pg/mL at 100 ng/mL. The CD4+ T + B-JAB condition (blue line) shows low Perforin release, remaining below 1500 pg/mL across the tested concentration range.
L1748: Figure 6 displays a dose-response graph showing the percentage of killing of endogenous B-cells from healthy donors versus B-JAB antibody concentration.
The Y-axis represents % of Killing (ranging from -20 to 90) and the X-axis represents Antibody (ng/mL) (log scale, 0.01 to 1000). Multiple cell lines are tested, including BJAB, DB, DoHH2, Nalm-6, SC-1, SU-DHL-8, WSU-DLCL2, and SU-DHL-1. All tested cell lines show dose-dependent killing, with EC50 values ranging from approximately 0.22 to 11 ng/mL, consistent with the text description.
L1749: Figure 7 displays a dose-response graph showing the percentage of autologous B-cell killing versus B-JAB antibody concentration in human CD20/CD3\epsilon double-transgenic B6N mice.

L1808: Model 178 sIL6R_All
Figure 57 displays nine plots arranged in a 3x3 grid, showing the Prediction-Corrected Visual Predictive Check (PCVPC) for the final model. The plots are divided into two main groups: Tocilizumab (upper row) and sIL-6R (lower row).
The columns represent different types of predictive checks:
Column 1 (DV vs PRED): Observed Data (DV) versus Predicted Data (PRED).
Column 2 (DV vs IPRED): Observed Data (DV) versus Individual Predicted Data (IPRED).
Column 3 (CMRES vs TIME): Conditional Mean Residual Error (CMRES) versus Time (day).
The rows represent different types of residuals:
Row 1 (Upper): Tocilizumab data.

L1810: Figure 58: Population Predictions of Final Model for a Typical Patient following Administration of Single 8 mg/kg IV Dose, by Disease. The graph plots Concentration (Y-axis, 0 to 800) versus Time (days) (X-axis, 0 to 8). The blue line represents the predicted concentration for patients with Rheumatoid Arthritis (RA). The red line represents the predicted concentration for patients with Cytopenia-Related Syndrome (CRS). Both lines show a rapid increase in concentration, peaking around 4 days, followed by a slight decline or stabilization. Shaded areas indicate the 95% prediction intervals (PIs). The blue PIs are wider and extend higher than the red PIs, indicating greater variability and higher predicted concentrations for the RA population.
L1811: Figure 59 displays four plots comparing drug concentration and receptor occupancy over time (day) for patients with CRS (black line) and RA (red line) following a single dose, based on population PK simulations.
Top Left Plot: Concentration (\mu\text{g/mL}) vs Time (day). Both curves show rapid initial decline, with the CRS curve maintaining a higher concentration for longer.
Top Right Plot: sIL6R Concentration (ng/mL) vs Time (day). Both curves peak around day 21-28, with the CRS curve peaking slightly higher.
Bottom Left Plot: sIL6R Receptor Occupancy (%) vs Time (day). Both curves rise sharply to 100% occupancy by day 7 and then decline, with the CRS curve declining faster.
Bottom Right Plot: sIL6R %free (%) vs Time (day). Both curves rise sharply from 0% to 100% free receptor by day 7 and then decline, with the CRS curve declining faster.
L1812: Box plot showing Maximum Concentration (\mu\text{g}/\text{mL}) versus Case (M8 to M24) for individual predictions following simulation scenarios. The Y-axis ranges from 100 to 700 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL}. A horizontal dashed red line indicates the Cmax HV = 649 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL}. The median maximum concentration for all cases is approximately 320 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL}, and the interquartile range (IQR) is generally between 300 and 400 \mu\text{g}/\text{mL}. All cases show maximum concentrations significantly below the Cmax HV threshold.
